3a:T1f56,# Relates to

Core mixed the runtime kernel with host configuration, domain DTOs, acoustic processing, assistant tests and application scripts. Private imports and forwarding modules obscured ownership and made independent cleanup difficult.

# Change

- Keep the Node runtime in `@elizaos/core` and portable kernel contracts in `@elizaos/core/protocol`. Internal modules import their defining files. JSON catalog assets keep explicit exports.
- Move HTTP, boot/configuration, registries, process policy and direct-media helpers to `@elizaos/host`; move domain DTOs, validation, LifeOps declarations and backup formats to `@elizaos/contracts`; move portable acoustic algorithms to `@elizaos/voice`. Core depends on none of those owners. Hosts explicitly compose assistant behavior, storage and providers.
- Migrate monorepo imports, mocks, dynamic types and build aliases together. Remove personal-assistant contract, app automation and UI domain/voice forwarding modules. Native-inference's existing optional local router remains an optional peer, avoiding a UI/provider dependency cycle.
- Fix conservative fact equality, immutable-image cache identity, deadline-factory rejection, awareness invalidation races, corrupt-registry errors and atomic registry publication. Preserve authorization, tenant isolation, cancellation, complete context, effect receipts and persisted backup bytes.
- Consolidate retry, scope predicates, migrations, mutation delegation, backup-reader cleanup, environment helpers and API-key hints. Keep model attempt/stream/failover settlement under its existing lifetime owner.
- Move assistant tests/live routing/latency tooling, identity merging and shared fixtures to their owners. Remove dead helpers, retired scripts, disabled APIs and redundant configuration. Exclude benchmark/generator sources from publication and verify installed tarballs.

Breaking import migration for the next release:

| Previous ownership | Public destination |
|---|---|
| Kernel implementation leaves | `@elizaos/core` or browser-safe `@elizaos/core/protocol` |
| Host configuration / HTTP / process helpers | `@elizaos/host` or browser-safe `@elizaos/host/protocol` |
| Domain and backup contracts | `@elizaos/contracts`; Node signing/verification uses `/node` |
| Portable acoustic processing | `@elizaos/voice` |
| Identity merge | `@elizaos/plugin-relationships` |
| Former `@elizaos/ui/types` forwarding entry | `@elizaos/contracts` |

These changes deliberately remove compatibility forwarding for retired imports. Voice algorithm bodies are unchanged after parsed-syntax comparison. Concurrent develop changes through `6084ff17cff4047e339fb01891b3c555c9a92fc2` are retained, including media cancellation, complete evaluator context and scoped SQLite reads.

41:T6c8,## Fix
A missing JavaScript callback left the WebView health observer permanently busy after its deadline. Replacing the WebView in the same Activity could also retain the old pending read. Retire the logical request on lifecycle changes, timeout, or interruption and fence late callbacks by request identity. Issued JavaScript itself is not cancellable.

The shared Android regression contract covers replacement without an old callback, deadline expiry, concurrent reads, late callbacks, navigation, pause/resume and destruction. Hosts retain their own origin, DOM policy and health-admission decisions.

48:T14f1,# Relates to

Existing approved phone [#30844](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/30844) and [#31025](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/31025).

# Change

Clock requests use the existing proposal, approval, claim and receipt flow. Owners review the exact request before separate native consent. Unknown results are reconciled without replay. Recurrence requires the negotiated v2 capability. A Clock-only executor exposes only operations it supports, while separately authorized app actions remain available.

The native instruction now defines the existing set, show, dismiss and snooze meanings under those capability checks. Show opens Android Clock without creating or changing an alarm. It is not generic app navigation, and it still requires native approval. Set may create or update an alarm; dismiss and snooze may change one. An opened receipt proves handoff, not final alarm state or ringing.

Exact action selection uses the existing DISCOVER_ACTIONS names interface. It does not request the nonexistent candidateActionNames discovery parameter or add an unconditional discovery stage.

# Why this implementation

The fix uses the existing capability, permission, consent, proposal and receipt gates. It adds guidance for current effects, not a new native effect. Native Java/Kotlin, manifest, dependencies and private custody remain unchanged. The source overlay retains MAIN/LAUNCHER; alarm/timer intent ownership and default Clock-role activation remain intentionally absent.

# Scope and testing

This update changes only context-assembly.ts and the existing real device-actions test from reviewed0d1189a99d6fe4ae20796b1ca6c60d0948549e20. The69 other PR files retain their bytes. The settled Clock UI and its owning test remain exact8020, already published atb176. The71-file scope is unchanged.

Owning qualification for this operation mapping passed77 tests, typecheck and lint. Earlier discovery qualification and native artifact proofs remain separate evidence. The27bf APK has an honest renderer, the same signer and16KiB alignment;181 classes,108 generated sources and690 non-renderer payloads remained exact.

A safe future Google Clock alarm was created through explicit native consent, read back and removed. The original two disabled alarms remained unchanged; native journal reached COMPLETE/OPENED. No ringing was tested. The separate foreground-return correction preserves settled results through refresh. The current live SHOW semantic retest is still active and is not yet accepted.

## How to test

1. Use the compatible enrolled Android app with the original owner and negotiated Clock capabilities.
2. Request SHOW through the Clock handoff operation. Review and approve it; confirm that it opens system Clock without creating or changing an alarm.
3. Test supported set/dismiss/snooze requests only with explicit device authority. Preserve exact repeat days; v1 must reject recurrence.
4. Return from external Clock. Check that a settled receipt remains visible through refresh and uncertain outcomes do not replay effects.
5. Verify an owned future canary and remove only that canary. Dispatch alone is insufficient proof of alarm state or ringing.

Auth mixed identity, provider probes, wallet signing, credential custody, native Cloud service composition, and duplicated transport/lifecycle code. ## Changes

- Flatten the nested server source trees; distinguish wallet signing from credential vault storage. Extract auth initialization and state ownership out of HTTP routes.

4c:Te69,# Relates to

Adds a comprehensive crypto terminal UI surface to the wallet plugin, enabling live market browsing, paper trading, price alerts, and Solana token safety checks.

# Change

Introduces a new **Crypto Terminal** view (`CryptoTerminalView.tsx`) alongside the existing wallet inventory, providing:

## Core Features
- **Live Markets**: Browse top-market-cap assets from CoinGecko with real-time price updates
- **Watchlist**: Curate a personal list of assets to monitor
- **Paper Trading**: Practice market and limit orders against live prices with a $10k starting balance
- **Price Alerts**: Set one-shot "above/below" price targets that fire once when met
- **Token Safety**: Run GoPlus Security checks on Solana token mints to assess risk (avoid/caution/no-major-flags verdicts)
- **Operating Modes**: HUNT (with scout ranking of 24h movers), SLEEP (live prices + alerts), OFF (manual refresh only)
- **Price Charts**: Interactive SVG line charts with pointer/keyboard navigation for 1D/1W/1M/3M/1Y periods

## Backend Routes
- `GET /api/wallet/terminal/markets` — CoinGecko top-market-cap list with caching and stale-data recovery
- `GET /api/wallet/terminal/chart?id=<id>&days=<1|7|30|90|365>` — Historical price data with per-period TTL
- `GET /api/wallet/terminal/token-safety?mint=<base58>` — GoPlus Solana token security report

## Implementation Details
- **Paper Ledger**: Pure state machine for practice cash, holdings, and orders; no wallet interaction
- **Persistence**: Paper state, watchlist, operating mode, and price alerts stored via `shellLocalStorage` under `eliza:wallet:` keys
- **Data Hooks**: `useTerminalMarkets`, `useTerminalChart`, `usePaperLedger`, `usePriceAlerts`, `useOperatingMode`, `useTokenSafety`, `useWatchlist`
- **Caching**: Concurrent-miss sharing, brief TTLs, stale-data fallback when refresh fails
- **Agent Integration**: New `token_safety` subaction under `WALLET` for agent-driven token checks; shipped `Crypto Queen` character with token-safety-first guidance

## Testing
- Comprehensive unit tests for paper ledger (fills, reservations, limit settlement, valuation)
- Operating mode state tests (parsing, transitions, scout ranking)
- Price alert tests (creation rules, one-shot triggering, OFF pause)
- Contract tests for market and token-safety routes (recorded CoinGecko/GoPlus payloads, adversarial variants, cache behavior)
- Full integration test of terminal UI (loading, search, watchlist, chart, orders, mode changes, alerts, token safety)
- Character schema validation test

62:T7ce,## Problem and change

Consumer apps currently duplicate the encrypted trace queue, consent-bound collector, HTTP transport, database lease and task-journal capture backend. Move those reusable hosts into `@elizaos/app/native-host` so products retain only their study measurement policy, console UI and explicit startup configuration.

The host requires an explicit measurement validator/report policy and explicit collection startup. It preserves the existing encrypted storage format, enrollment/role checks, consent and withdrawal, retention, key rotation, exact upload acknowledgement, persistent retry/deduplication, owner fencing and structural-only task evidence. Transport uses caller cancellation; stopping capture aborts an unanswered request before releasing its database lease. An old lease owner cannot unlink a replacement owner's lease.

#33275 has merged and supplies the native-host entry point and typed errors. This PR now contains only the shared research collector/trace changes and targets `develop`.

Updates `multidict` from 6.7.1 to 6.9.1
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/aio-libs/multidict/releases">multidict's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>6.9.1</h2>
<h2>Bug fixes</h2>
<ul>
<li>
<p>Fixed the C extension reading freed memory on free-threaded builds when a
list handed to :py:meth:<code>~multidict.MultiDict.update</code>,
:py:meth:<code>~multidict.MultiDict.extend</code>, :py:meth:<code>~multidict.MultiDict.merge</code>
or the :py:class:<code>~multidict.MultiDict</code> and :py:class:<code>~multidict.CIMultiDict</code>
constructors, a <code>[key, value]</code> item inside any iterable handed to them, or a
list tested with <code>in</code> against :py:meth:<code>~multidict.MultiDict.items</code>, is
changed by another thread; a call that catches the list shrinking under it
now raises :py:exc:<code>RuntimeError</code> -- by :user:<code>rodrigobnogueira</code>.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em>
<a href="https://redirect.github.com/aio-libs/multidict/issues/1437">#1437</a>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed a data race on the free-threaded build where a retired hash table's
reader count used relaxed atomics, letting a lock-free <code>get()</code>/<code>getone()</code>/
<code>__getitem__()</code> read race a concurrent free of that table. The reader-exit
decrement and the drain's free check now use release/acquire ordering
instead -- by :user:<code>asvetlov</code>.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em>
<a href="https://redirect.github.com/aio-libs/multidict/issues/1481">#1481</a>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed a free-threaded build bug where two threads calling <code>update()</code>,
<code>merge()</code>, or <code>__setitem__()</code> on the same key at the same time could lose
the key entirely instead of just racing on which value wins. A decref of the
replaced value could transiently suspend the writer's critical section,
letting a second writer for the same key observe the first writer's
in-progress entry as absent and, once both settled, mistake it for a stale
duplicate and delete it. Every such decref is now deferred until the writer
has released its critical section, so the window can no longer open.
<code>setdefault()</code> had an unrelated instance of the same blind spot (it could
insert a duplicate rather than recognizing an in-flight key), fixed alongside
it -- by :user:<code>asvetlov</code>.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em>
<a href="https://redirect.github.com/aio-libs/multidict/issues/1483">#1483</a>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed a free-threaded build bug where <code>getall()</code> and the <code>items()</code>/
<code>keys()</code>/<code>values()</code> equality path could raise <code>KeyError</code> or report a
present, never-deleted key as missing. A concurrent <code>update()</code>/<code>extend()</code>/
<code>__setitem__()</code> call can have its critical section transiently suspended
(a decref triggering a blocking allocator call) while an entry is marked as
part of its own bookkeeping; a reader landing in that window used to treat
the mark as &quot;not found&quot; instead of &quot;still there, in flight&quot; -- by
:user:<code>asvetlov</code>.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em></p>
</li>
</ul>
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
</blockquote>
<p>... (truncated)</p>
</details>

(a decref triggering a blocking allocator call) while an entry is marked as</p>

Updates `werkzeug` from 3.1.8 to 3.1.9
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/pallets/werkzeug/releases">werkzeug's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>3.1.9</h2>
<p>This is the Werkzeug 3.1.9 security fix release, which fixes security issues and bugs but does not otherwise change behavior and should not result in breaking changes compared to the latest feature release.</p>
<p>PyPI: <a href="https://pypi.org/project/Werkzeug/3.1.9/">https://pypi.org/project/Werkzeug/3.1.9/</a>
Changes: <a href="https://werkzeug.palletsprojects.com/page/changes/#version-3-1-9">https://werkzeug.palletsprojects.com/page/changes/#version-3-1-9</a>
Milestone: <a href="https://github.com/pallets/werkzeug/milestone/46?closed=1">https://github.com/pallets/werkzeug/milestone/46?closed=1</a></p>
<ul>
<li><code>safe_join</code> on Windows does not allow special devices names with empty ADS markers on NTFS. <a href="https://github.com/pallets/werkzeug/security/advisories/GHSA-g6x2-hccm-hh4m">https://github.com/pallets/werkzeug/security/advisories/GHSA-g6x2-hccm-hh4m</a></li>
<li><code>ProfilerMiddleware</code> uses <code>profiling.tracing</code> on Python 3.15. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3207">#3207</a></li>
<li><code>uri_to_iri</code> and <code>iri_to_uri</code> preserve empty username, password, and port 0. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3189">#3189</a></li>
<li>Improve performance of <code>parse_options_header</code>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3231">#3231</a></li>
<li>Improve performance of <code>parse_etags</code>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3231">#3231</a></li>
<li>Improve performance of <code>parse_cookie</code>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3231">#3231</a></li>
<li><code>get_host</code> also checks that the port is in the valid range. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3236">#3236</a></li>
<li>The <code>int</code> URL converter returns a 404 instead of 500 error when the value is longer than <code>sys.get_int_max_str_digits()</code>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3237">#3237</a></li>
<li>Improve debugger PIN generation from cgroup data inside Podman. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3245">#3245</a></li>
<li><code>Authorization</code> parsing <code>basic</code> auth disallows non-base64 characters.  <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3248">#3248</a></li>
<li><code>application/x-www-form-urlencoded</code> form data is no longer limited to <code>max_form_memory_size</code>, only <code>max_content_length</code>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3251">#3251</a></li>
<li><code>LimitedStream.readinto</code> does not resize the buffer when it reads less than the remaining size. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3253">#3253</a></li>
<li>Rules with 10 or more converters in a single part assign matched values correctly. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3254">#3254</a></li>
<li>The invalid <code>Range</code> suffix length <code>-0</code> is no longer accepted. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3255">#3255</a></li>
</ul>
</blockquote>
</details>
<details>
<summary>Changelog</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/pallets/werkzeug/blob/main/CHANGES.rst">werkzeug's changelog</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>Version 3.1.9</h2>
<p>Released 2026-09-27</p>
<ul>
<li><code>safe_join</code> on Windows does not allow special devices names with empty ADS
markers on NTFS. :ghsa:<code>g6x2-hccm-hh4m</code></li>

:issue:<code>3207</code></li>
<li><code>uri_to_iri</code> and <code>iri_to_uri</code> preserve empty username, password, and
port 0. :issue:<code>3189</code></li>
<li>Improve performance of <code>parse_options_header</code>. :pr:<code>3231</code></li>
<li>Improve performance of <code>parse_etags</code>. :pr:<code>3231</code></li>
<li>Improve performance of <code>parse_cookie</code>. :pr:<code>3231</code></li>
<li><code>get_host</code> also checks that the port is in the valid range. :pr:<code>3236</code></li>
<li>The <code>int</code> URL converter returns a 404 instead of 500 error when the value
is longer than <code>sys.get_int_max_str_digits()</code>. :issue:<code>3237</code></li>
<li>Improve debugger PIN generation from cgroup data inside Podman.
:issue:<code>3245</code></li>
<li><code>Authorization</code> parsing <code>basic</code> auth disallows non-base64 characters.
:pr:<code>3248</code></li>
<li><code>application/x-www-form-urlencoded</code> form data is no longer limited to
<code>max_form_memory_size</code>, only <code>max_content_length</code>. :pr:<code>3251</code></li>
<li><code>LimitedStream.readinto</code> does not resize the buffer when it reads less
than the remaining size. :pr:<code>3253</code></li>
<li>Rules with 10 or more converters in a single part assign matched values
correctly. :pr:<code>3254</code></li>

</ul>

Updates `multidict` from 6.7.1 to 6.9.1
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/aio-libs/multidict/releases">multidict's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>6.9.1</h2>
<h2>Bug fixes</h2>
<ul>
<li>
<p>Fixed the C extension reading freed memory on free-threaded builds when a
list handed to :py:meth:<code>~multidict.MultiDict.update</code>,
:py:meth:<code>~multidict.MultiDict.extend</code>, :py:meth:<code>~multidict.MultiDict.merge</code>
or the :py:class:<code>~multidict.MultiDict</code> and :py:class:<code>~multidict.CIMultiDict</code>
constructors, a <code>[key, value]</code> item inside any iterable handed to them, or a
list tested with <code>in</code> against :py:meth:<code>~multidict.MultiDict.items</code>, is
changed by another thread; a call that catches the list shrinking under it
now raises :py:exc:<code>RuntimeError</code> -- by :user:<code>rodrigobnogueira</code>.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em>
<a href="https://redirect.github.com/aio-libs/multidict/issues/1437">#1437</a>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed a data race on the free-threaded build where a retired hash table's
reader count used relaxed atomics, letting a lock-free <code>get()</code>/<code>getone()</code>/
<code>__getitem__()</code> read race a concurrent free of that table. The reader-exit
decrement and the drain's free check now use release/acquire ordering
instead -- by :user:<code>asvetlov</code>.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em>
<a href="https://redirect.github.com/aio-libs/multidict/issues/1481">#1481</a>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed a free-threaded build bug where two threads calling <code>update()</code>,
<code>merge()</code>, or <code>__setitem__()</code> on the same key at the same time could lose
the key entirely instead of just racing on which value wins. A decref of the
replaced value could transiently suspend the writer's critical section,
letting a second writer for the same key observe the first writer's
in-progress entry as absent and, once both settled, mistake it for a stale
duplicate and delete it. Every such decref is now deferred until the writer
has released its critical section, so the window can no longer open.
<code>setdefault()</code> had an unrelated instance of the same blind spot (it could
insert a duplicate rather than recognizing an in-flight key), fixed alongside
it -- by :user:<code>asvetlov</code>.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em>
<a href="https://redirect.github.com/aio-libs/multidict/issues/1483">#1483</a>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Fixed a free-threaded build bug where <code>getall()</code> and the <code>items()</code>/
<code>keys()</code>/<code>values()</code> equality path could raise <code>KeyError</code> or report a
present, never-deleted key as missing. A concurrent <code>update()</code>/<code>extend()</code>/
<code>__setitem__()</code> call can have its critical section transiently suspended
(a decref triggering a blocking allocator call) while an entry is marked as
part of its own bookkeeping; a reader landing in that window used to treat
the mark as &quot;not found&quot; instead of &quot;still there, in flight&quot; -- by
:user:<code>asvetlov</code>.</p>
<p><em>Related issues and pull requests on GitHub:</em></p>
</li>
</ul>
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
</blockquote>
<p>... (truncated)</p>
</details>

Updates `werkzeug` from 3.1.8 to 3.1.9
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/pallets/werkzeug/releases">werkzeug's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>3.1.9</h2>
<p>This is the Werkzeug 3.1.9 security fix release, which fixes security issues and bugs but does not otherwise change behavior and should not result in breaking changes compared to the latest feature release.</p>
<p>PyPI: <a href="https://pypi.org/project/Werkzeug/3.1.9/">https://pypi.org/project/Werkzeug/3.1.9/</a>
Changes: <a href="https://werkzeug.palletsprojects.com/page/changes/#version-3-1-9">https://werkzeug.palletsprojects.com/page/changes/#version-3-1-9</a>
Milestone: <a href="https://github.com/pallets/werkzeug/milestone/46?closed=1">https://github.com/pallets/werkzeug/milestone/46?closed=1</a></p>
<ul>
<li><code>safe_join</code> on Windows does not allow special devices names with empty ADS markers on NTFS. <a href="https://github.com/pallets/werkzeug/security/advisories/GHSA-g6x2-hccm-hh4m">https://github.com/pallets/werkzeug/security/advisories/GHSA-g6x2-hccm-hh4m</a></li>
<li><code>ProfilerMiddleware</code> uses <code>profiling.tracing</code> on Python 3.15. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3207">#3207</a></li>
<li><code>uri_to_iri</code> and <code>iri_to_uri</code> preserve empty username, password, and port 0. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3189">#3189</a></li>
<li>Improve performance of <code>parse_options_header</code>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3231">#3231</a></li>
<li>Improve performance of <code>parse_etags</code>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3231">#3231</a></li>
<li>Improve performance of <code>parse_cookie</code>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3231">#3231</a></li>
<li><code>get_host</code> also checks that the port is in the valid range. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3236">#3236</a></li>
<li>The <code>int</code> URL converter returns a 404 instead of 500 error when the value is longer than <code>sys.get_int_max_str_digits()</code>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3237">#3237</a></li>
<li>Improve debugger PIN generation from cgroup data inside Podman. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3245">#3245</a></li>
<li><code>Authorization</code> parsing <code>basic</code> auth disallows non-base64 characters.  <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3248">#3248</a></li>
<li><code>application/x-www-form-urlencoded</code> form data is no longer limited to <code>max_form_memory_size</code>, only <code>max_content_length</code>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3251">#3251</a></li>
<li><code>LimitedStream.readinto</code> does not resize the buffer when it reads less than the remaining size. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3253">#3253</a></li>
<li>Rules with 10 or more converters in a single part assign matched values correctly. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3254">#3254</a></li>
<li>The invalid <code>Range</code> suffix length <code>-0</code> is no longer accepted. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/pallets/werkzeug/issues/3255">#3255</a></li>
</ul>
</blockquote>
</details>

</details>83:T5a7,The isolated Android runner only accepted successful instrumentation, preventing consumers from qualifying whole-app interruption without bypassing shared ownership and cleanup. Add an explicit interrupted phase after successful setup, followed by a required separate successful recovery phase.

The harness owns a fresh run nonce and binds the private readiness marker to the installed package UID, Android user, main PID and process start time. It refuses UIDs shared with other packages and rechecks the lease and installed APK hashes, force-stops only that owned package, and requires all observed UID processes to exit. A matching started-but-crashed instrumentation result is recorded separately from passing JUnit evidence. Stale markers, process replacement, lost custody, early exit, timeout and overlapping phases fail. Cleanup cannot satisfy the recovery requirement.

Validation: all 101 process/marker/supervisor/parser and isolated-runner regressions passed at the initial PR source; all 9 affected mechanism/integration cases passed after adding the exclusive-UID refusal. Focused formatting/lint passes. Repository-wide verification passes all 298 tasks and subsequent audits at 628da596dcd9. Hosted checks remain pending. No installed Android or consumer recovery acceptance is claimed. The Senior Care native qualifier must adopt this API after review and still requires authorized account sign-in and its pinned custom browser.
84:T564,Native hosts previously needed consumer-local code to manage subscriptions, and their undo transport could not bind a reversal to reviewed renewal terms. Move the management, restricted portal and lifecycle broker into the shared Cloud SDK native entrypoint; require the reviewed digest for undo and call the durable confirmation API merged in #33342.

Request identities survive native restarts and bind approved terms plus an optional confirmed FAILED predecessor. Changed terms cannot collide with an inaccessible earlier failed intent. Pending/status recovery stays read-only; the server still owns billing authorization, pending-command exclusion and durable dispatch. Existing SDK service-only host policy and account-generation fences are retained. The public projection strips provider fields and validates the complete short-lived estimate.

Validation: 21 synthetic native tests pass, including account changes while authority resolves, private-token transport, failed retry, lost response followed by changed terms, restart identity, read-only recovery and portal origin restrictions. SDK build/typecheck, workflow lint, diff checks and commit-range secret scanning pass. Root verification is running; no deployed provider or installed consumer acceptance is claimed. The published-files manifest includes the projection module and PR validation runs the native suite.
85:T498,Native JSON transports currently wait for complete speech clips and cannot carry Cloud character timing. Add start/pull/cancel sessions over one Cloud stream, retaining original and normalized alignment and replaying the last cursor after a lost reply without another synthesis. Host policy owns voice identity and context screening; account changes abort streams and fence retained audio.

Sessions are memory-only, bounded and expire after ten minutes. Cancellation by request identity can overtake start. Legacy MP3 responses stay on the original request with null timing. The README documents shutdown cleanup, capacity and the restart/expiry boundary; this change does not itself implement client playback.

Validation: 46 native-host tests, including real loopback HTTP streaming before EOF, duplicate starts/pulls, cancellation, logout, credential replacement, strict admission and legacy response handling; SDK typecheck. Final clean committed-source root verification passes all 298 tasks and final audits. The 8 MiB audio-bound regression also covers a corrected regular-expression stack-exhaustion failure. No live provider synthesis or device playback is claimed.
86:T8b8,External Android apps currently carry duplicate Gradle projects or copy a full product platform tree. Add an owned generated consumer host under the existing app mobile tooling. The host supplies identity, distribution variants, manifests/resources, explicit native modules and source selections; no other product UI or service tree is copied.

The generator validates declared source roots, rejects traversal/symlink escapes and refuses unowned projects or generated-file collisions. Its ownership marker permits regeneration and removal of formerly selected generated files while preserving unrelated build outputs. The compatibility toolchain retains reviewed Gradle 8.13, AGP 8.13, Kotlin 2.2.20 and JDK 21 instead of silently adopting the full app's Gradle 9.5 wrapper. Distribution and wrapper bytes are hash pinned; optional verified native payloads use the existing exact-byte packaging helper.

Validation: ten filesystem/profile contracts pass. SDK qualification builds eight debug/release APKs across two independent packages, checks literal application labels, standalone/launcher resource overlays and HOME manifest differences. A synthetic non-executable runtime payload checks inclusion of bundle/inventory assets and exclusion of ABI asset trees. This exposed AGP ignoring source-directory asset exclusions; the host now uses a dependent Sync staging task. Receipts: test-results/android-consumer-host/2026-10-04T06-23-04-985Z/verification.json. A Senior Care generated-project preview also built standalone/launcher debug, release, test APKs and lint (630 Gradle tasks). Full root verification passes on 47d7a434cf: all 287 tasks and final package/test-lane audits.

87:T8fb,## Problem and change

External Android hosts still needed a downstream patch to prepare OTA updates with authenticated clock bounds and durable authorization. Move those contracts into native-agent: hosts supply trust, current installed identity and clock/configuration ports; preparation preserves cancellation, locking and journal admission. No signer, trust enrollment, discovery loop or installer authority is enabled by default.

Add a content-addressed OTA source composer with strict host policy, collision checks and cache verification, alongside the existing shared Android AAR builder. Add a committed-source mobile DNS bundler so thin consumers do not need a monorepo dependency installation or stale copies of the core/host dependency closure. Fix the research server's asset-error response by reading before committing success headers.

The Android candidate does not establish ARM64 agent execution, production signing/enrollment, AOSP boot or physical-device acceptance.

## Review fixes

At 9b9f3b3e01, DNS verification independently rebuilds from the reviewed commit and compares the executable hash. It rejects coupled executable/sidecar tampering and hashes committed sources instead of the working tree. The real-build regression includes that attack. The newer upstream gateway artifact owner is reused with host-selected layout and DNS callbacks, covered by a deployed bootstrap import test. The README merge conflict is resolved.

The initial root check reached its 6 GiB cgroup memory limit. The final-head rerun passed, bounded to 8 GiB with single-task concurrency.
88:T6f1,Consumer Android hosts still had to implement child-process supervision, rewrite mobile DNS imports, and duplicate development/runtime and packaged-document provenance checks. This change moves those mechanisms to their Eliza owners so hosts supply identity, policy, skills, gateway composition and acceptance scenarios.

- Add `NativeProcessSupervisor` to the native-agent runtime with launch epochs, readiness deadlines, direct-child resource ownership, bounded restart policy and cancellation fences. The canonical Android service also uses its graceful termination helper.
- Export small mobile DNS dependencies directly so consumers can bundle the reviewed source without text rewriting.
- Add pinned runtime build/staging orchestration and development source inspection to app tooling, and verify final APK document bytes through the existing document owner.

Validation: portable native-host suite passes, including 34 process supervisor assertions and existing real process-death suites; six Android supervisor instrumentation tests pass across both consumer distributions on an owned API35 x86_64 emulator under enforcing SELinux, with package cleanup and HOME unchanged; both candidate APKs, test APKs and lint build successfully. Four focused provenance/document tests and the real mobile DNS integration pass. Root `bun run verify` passes all 287 tasks and the final repository audits.

The Android instrumentation establishes direct-child supervision under the app UID. It does not establish ARM64 Bun/foreground-service, inference, AOSP or physical-device acceptance. Non-cooperating readiness callbacks cannot publish late readiness, but must still terminate their own external I/O; detached descendant processes are outside this direct-child helper's contract.
89:T7f3,## Problem and behavior
The private native billing broker could manage cancellation and renewal, but could not review or confirm an upgrade/downgrade, rediscover its original command after restart, or continue its original upgrade payment. Add native plan-change review, confirmation, status, pending discovery and payment routes using the existing private billing session and account epoch.

The native boundary validates and projects public financial terms, command/effect state, paginated source/lease observations and temporary original-invoice payment continuations. Plan keys and currency come from host policy; the current review contract explicitly supports monthly plans. Provider payloads and credentials are stripped. Confirmation keys derive from original quote and action across restarts; status/discovery never submit confirmation. Changed accounts fence late responses, malformed/expired reviews reject, and payment URLs must be unexpired canonical Stripe hosted invoice addresses for an unresolved original upgrade. Hosted URLs are not stored or logged by this broker.

## Validation
- Full SDK suite: 69 Bun cases / 1,442 assertions plus 31 native-host cases pass.
- New real-loopback native tests cover both review types, renderer-supplied authority rejection, private session use, stable restart keys, lost response followed by read-only discovery/status, payment continuation, malformed responses and late account changes. Projection negatives cover monetary/date/state/URL validation and private-field stripping.
- Full local root verification: 298 tasks plus final audits, exit 0 in 153.0 seconds on clean unchanged head `91f29c0ea27d252d907fe2a2fd9660aefa9fcea7`.
- SDK typecheck, scoped Biome and diff checks pass.
- Actual local npm package includes the new source module, and its extracted native entrypoint imports successfully.

Native product overlay/UI adoption, installed Android behavior, deployment and live-provider/device/user acceptance remain separate gates. This PR does not claim those outcomes.
8a:T624,After a quote is consumed, organization upgrades need exclusive execution ownership and an explicit boundary before any provider mutation. Initialize ready provenance at admission, acquire original-actor execution leases, revalidate source and reviewed terms before dispatch, and fence stale lease callbacks. Expired unstarted commands terminate so users can request a fresh review. Started uncertain commands remain read-only recovery candidates and cannot be reset or failed as unstarted.

This follows merged PR33506. It does not issue a provider mutation, expose public confirmation, publish paid allowance, implement scheduled downgrades or integrate the consumer UI. The paid-invoice verifier checks original invoice and price binding, complete proration lines, exact reviewed totals and settled payment state. It produces an observation only; durable dispatch receipts, provider execution/recovery and atomic finalization remain required.
